# Comprehensive Project Review

- Review started: 2026-07-19 01:20:35 CEST
- Repository branch: `quantum-computing-book`
- Intended scope: every project file, reviewed from start to finish
- Current status: in progress; the excessive-granularity second pass is complete through Chapter 10
- Method: findings are appended immediately after each smallest practical review unit (normally a sub-subsection, code/configuration unit, or individual asset).
- Review dimensions: correctness, pedagogy, prose and structure, consistency, maintainability, accessibility, reader entertainment, slight suspicions, unresolved doubts, and incremental improvement ideas.

## Review conventions

- **Severity:** critical, major, moderate, minor, suspicion, or polish.
- **Entertainment:** 1/5 (actively dry or frustrating), 2/5 (functional but flat), 3/5 (solid), 4/5 (engaging), 5/5 (exceptionally compelling).
- “No defect found” means the unit was inspected; it does not claim mathematical proof or external experimental verification.
- Archived material is evaluated for archival usefulness, labeling, provenance, and risk of confusing maintainers—not as if it were current book copy.

## Review log

### Repository-wide verification baseline

#### Manuscript structural lint

- **Result:** `make lint` passes all 48 manuscript files.
- **Positive:** The status blocks, navigation, headings, sanctioned bridge exceptions, notation
  restrictions, and source-detectable Markdown/math invariants have a working automated guardrail.
- **Residual doubt (minor):** A clean structural lint result is easy to overread as editorial or factual
  cleanliness. The command's success message should perhaps say “structural/style checks” rather than
  merely “files checked,” especially because the claim-anchor audit currently fails substantially.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Invisible to ordinary readers but valuable because clean rendering prevents
  avoidable friction.

#### Executable examples

- **Result:** `make check-examples` passes all four programs: Deutsch–Jozsa, Bell state, Grover, and
  statevector simulation.
- **Positive:** The outputs are plausible and pedagogically legible; each program prints a human-readable
  interpretation rather than silently exiting.
- **Suspicion:** The Bell counts and Grover counts are nondeterministic. This is appropriate physics, but
  deterministic seeds or tolerant assertions are worth confirming inside the harness so a rare statistical
  fluctuation cannot create a flaky check.
- **Polish:** The setup target upgraded `pip` and installed a large figure-generation dependency set merely
  to run four examples. A separate minimal example dependency file/environment would make verification
  faster and less surprising.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The compact observed distributions make abstract claims concrete.

#### Fact-check card lint

- **Result:** `python3 scripts/factcheck_lint.py` reports 0 problems but inspects only 3 cards in 1 file.
- **Major concern:** The repository contains a large per-chapter fact-check mirror, so this default invocation
  gives a dangerously reassuring green result with extremely narrow coverage. The CLI should either scan the
  complete mirror by default or state prominently that a path argument is required.
- **Entertainment: 1/5.** This is maintainer infrastructure, and the misleadingly narrow success harms trust.

#### Fact-check anchor audit

- **Result:** `python3 scripts/factcheck_anchors.py` fails with **97 stale anchors across 52 files**.
- **Major defect:** The fact-check mirror no longer reliably points to the current manuscript wording.
  Staleness spans front matter, glossary, most technical parts, and numerous perishable hardware/software
  claims. Until repaired, the mirror cannot support the README's implication that flagged claims have been
  externally verified in their current textual form.
- **Moderate defect:** Python emits `SyntaxWarning: invalid escape sequence '\;'` at line 2 of the checker.
  Make the module docstring raw or double the backslash; warnings in a verification tool reduce confidence.
- **Priority:** Repair anchors before promoting any file from `prereviewed`; distinguish harmless wording drift
  from claims whose substance changed, and re-open source verification for the latter.
- **Entertainment: 1/5.** Readers do not see this directly, but broken provenance removes the pleasure of
  trusting a technically ambitious book.

### Top-level project documents

#### `INSTRUCTIONS.md` — Purpose

- **No substantive defect found.** The scope is short and clear.
- **Minor improvement:** Say whether these instructions apply only to changes or also to review-only artifacts;
  the current wording leaves that boundary implicit.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.** Appropriately utilitarian.

#### `INSTRUCTIONS.md` — Git identity

- **Moderate concern:** Requiring contributors to alter their *global* Git identity is unusually invasive and
  unnecessary for guaranteeing this repository's commits; repo-local identity is sufficient. It also conflicts
  with normal multi-project workflows.
- **Improvement:** Require repo-local identity and document global configuration only as an optional personal
  default.
- **Entertainment: 1/5.** Pure administration, with a small moment of alarm for contributors.

#### `INSTRUCTIONS.md` — Commit rules and verification

- **Positive:** Exact author/committer requirements and verification commands remove ambiguity.
- **Suspicion:** The rules do not explain how co-authorship, patches from outside contributors, bots, or signed
  commits should preserve provenance. Forcing the maintainer's identity onto every contributed commit could
  misrepresent authorship.
- **Improvement:** State the provenance policy explicitly and add a non-destructive pre-commit verification
  target if this identity constraint is essential.
- **Entertainment: 1/5.**

#### `STYLE.md` — File and directory layout

- **Positive:** Concrete paths and numeric ordering make the architecture immediately recoverable.
- **Minor doubt:** The rule says one chapter per Markdown file but the Historical Prelude deliberately has
  multiple H1 episode units. Mentioning that exception here as well as later would reduce initial uncertainty.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.**

#### `STYLE.md` — Per-chapter structure and closing conventions

- **Positive:** This is unusually precise institutional memory. The bridge exceptions and section-count rules
  prevent well-intentioned “normalization” from destroying deliberate structure.
- **Minor concern:** The paragraph is dense enough that a small normative table (“required / allowed exception /
  lint source”) would be easier to audit.
- **Foggy thought:** A growing named-exception list in prose and `tools/lint.py` risks dual-source drift. Consider
  one machine-readable manifest imported by both documentation generation and lint.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.** Dry, but the history of seven competing closing styles adds human texture.

#### `STYLE.md` — Historical Prelude conventions

- **Positive:** It explicitly protects intentional local style from generic cleanup.
- **Suspicion:** “Renderer-bug-proof” is absolute language. “Robust against the renderer bugs currently tracked
  by this repository” would age better.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** The rationale makes an exception feel purposeful rather than arbitrary.

#### `STYLE.md` — Math

- **Positive:** The escape rules are concrete, empirically motivated, and backed by a canonical test matrix.
- **Moderate usability concern:** Source text with doubled escapes is hostile to authors and can be easy to copy
  incorrectly into non-GitHub contexts. A formatter or preprocessor could preserve clean authoring syntax while
  generating GitHub-safe output.
- **Minor concern:** “renders natively on all” overstates portability because renderer versions and security
  policies change. Date or version the compatibility statement.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.** Essential but cognitively taxing; a before/after visual would improve it.

#### `STYLE.md` — Notation defaults

- **No defect found.** The defaults are conventional and concise.
- **Polish:** Cross-link the notation appendix so maintainers can jump from normative rules to the reader-facing
  reference.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.**

#### `STYLE.md` — Markdown rules

- **Positive:** The rules favor portable source and clean diffs.
- **Minor ambiguity:** “Use blockquotes for definitions, theorems, and asides” conflicts slightly with the later
  insistence that sanity checks not be blockquoted; the exception is documented elsewhere but a cross-reference
  here would help.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.**

#### `STYLE.md` — Cross-references

- **Positive:** The selective-reader principle is excellent and directly reader-centered.
- **Foggy thought:** Deliberate repetition can become visible redundancy for linear readers. A review heuristic
  could ask whether repeated context is one sentence or an entire re-explanation.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** The philosophy promises a less frustrating technical book.

#### `STYLE.md` — Diagrams

- **Positive:** Regenerability, committed SVG, previews, and mandatory alt text form a sound pipeline.
- **Minor concern:** The section names Mermaid as an option while admitting incomplete Pandoc portability; the
  project should either lint against Mermaid in manuscript files or define the exact exception policy.
- **Accessibility improvement:** Alt text requirements should distinguish concise semantic descriptions from
  captions and should require long descriptions for dense plots/circuits where necessary.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Visual variety is a major engagement lever, though the policy itself is procedural.

#### `STYLE.md` — Authorship and history

- **Concern:** This repeats the identity mandate without addressing actual contributor provenance; see
  `INSTRUCTIONS.md`.
- **Entertainment: 1/5.**

#### `README.md` — Title, pitch, audience, and reading paths

#### `README.md` — Table of contents

#### `BookDescription.md` — Manuscript reconciliation note

- **Minor defect:** “remain accurate. the original planned outline” begins a new sentence with lowercase “the.”

#### `BookDescription.md` — Description, audience, and assumed background

- **Positive:** Audience definition is unusually concrete and helps readers self-select.

#### `BookDescription.md` — What the book is not and core philosophy

- **Positive:** The anti-metaphor, anti-hype stance is distinctive and credible.
- **Minor suspicion:** Repeated “not” framing can feel combative. Pair each rejection immediately with the

- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Strong editorial personality, though repetition slightly blunts it.

#### `PROGRESS.md` — Instructions and summary

- **Major defect:** Every file is `prereviewed`, a status explicitly later than `draft`, yet the generated summary

- **Likely cause:** `scripts/generate_progress.py` probably uses an outdated status-order comparison or treats
  only literal `draft`/`reviewed`/`final` as complete and omits `prereviewed`.
- **Improvement:** Fix the generator, add a test covering every lifecycle state, regenerate this file, and rename
  “completed” if it is intended to mean a narrower milestone.
- **Entertainment: 1/5.** A progress dashboard that contradicts itself is actively frustrating.

#### `PROGRESS.md` — Per-phase entries

- **Positive:** The phase organization explains dependency-aware drafting and every entry links directly to the

- **Minor concern:** All unchecked boxes visually imply “not done,” despite `prereviewed` status and full section

- **Entertainment: 2/5.** Scannable, but the checkbox semantics currently undermine satisfaction.

### Front matter

#### `book/00-front-matter/00-preface.md` — Why This Book Exists

- **Positive:** The two-reputations opening creates immediate tension, then resolves it into the book's
  distinctive “young engineering discipline” thesis. The audience and missing-middle argument are unusually
  concrete.
- **Minor suspicion:** The claim that software stacks are “converging fast enough” is both perishable and less
  well-defined than the surrounding prose. Name the layer that is converging (IRs, circuit APIs, runtime model)
  or soften it.
- **Polish:** The paragraph comparing this book with Nielsen–Chuang risks sounding like the canonical text is
  deficient rather than differently scoped. The qualifications mostly fix this, but the publication-date point
  could be phrased more generously.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Clear conflict, authorial stake, and a concrete promise make this an excellent opening.

#### `book/00-front-matter/00-preface.md` — Who This Book Is For

#### `book/00-front-matter/02-notation-and-conventions.md` — N.2 Matrix Notation

- **Positive:** The “type-signature bug” treatment of bars is unusually clear.

- **Positive:** Thorough without becoming a derivation chapter.
- **Minor defect:** “the parser disambiguates by the closing delimiter” anthropomorphizes rendering; it is the

#### `book/00-front-matter/02-notation-and-conventions.md` — N.4 Tensor Products

- **Positive:** The explicit book-to-Qiskit mapping is essential and concrete.
- **Maintenance concern:** Ordering explanations recur in many files and several corresponding fact-check anchors

- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### `book/00-front-matter/02-notation-and-conventions.md` — N.5 Circuit Notation

- **Positive:** Clear separation of top-wire numbering from bit-string significance avoids a classic trap.
- **Minor accessibility thought:** Provide a single labeled circuit image adjacent to this symbol inventory;
  prose-only glyph definitions ask readers to visualize too much.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### `book/00-front-matter/02-notation-and-conventions.md` — N.6 Probability and Measurement

- **Positive:** Classical-to-quantum progression and explicit Born-rule variants make this useful as a reference.
- **Minor notation concern:** `$X\perp Y$` for independence can be confused with orthogonality in this exact
  subject. `$X\mathrel{\perp\!\!\!\perp}Y$` is less ambiguous if renderer-safe.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### `book/00-front-matter/02-notation-and-conventions.md` — N.7 Callouts, references, and sources

- **Positive:** Making epistemic/status conventions reader-visible strengthens trust.
- **Suspicion:** Verify that every callout style described here is actually linted or intentionally advisory;
  pseudo-normative prose that tools do not enforce tends to drift.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.**

## Second pass — live book, excessive-granularity review

- Started: 2026-07-19 CEST
- Scope: the live manuscript under `book/`, processed in reading order.
- Isolation rule: only one manuscript file is opened at a time. Within that file, headings are inventoried and
  individual sections are read separately before their notes are appended.
- This pass does not inherit a “clean” judgment from the first pass. It reconsiders correctness, qualification,
  hidden assumptions, local transitions, selective-reader self-containment, examples, rhythm, accessibility,
  and entertainment at the smallest meaningful textual unit.

### `book/part-00-historical-prelude/00-historical-prelude.md` — second pass

#### File header, navigation, opening contract, and episode map (lines 1–26)

- **Structure:** Status, section count, and navigation are internally coherent. Calling this “the only chapter
  you can safely skip” is effective orientation, although technically the Prelude is unnumbered and elsewhere is
  distinguished from the chapters; “the only major narrative unit” would remove that tiny taxonomy wobble.
- **Reader contract:** “Why the model has the shape it does” is a strong promise and the four-step causal arc is
  stated twice: first in the reading callout, then in the bold thesis and developer analogy. The second statement
  adds value, but the adjacent repetition slightly delays Episode I. One can trim the callout or the bold paragraph
  without losing navigation.
- **Selective reading:** The claim that each episode can be “entered cold” sits uneasily beside “Read linearly”
  and the claim that every episode hands off causally. Clarify: episodes are locally intelligible but the argument
  compounds when read linearly.
- **Analogy precision:** The software lifecycle analogy is memorable, but “edge cases appear” can subtly frame
  experimental falsification as if nature exposed implementation bugs. It works rhetorically; one sentence could
  explicitly say the analogy stops there—the physical model, not nature, was defective.
- **Style suspicion:** “Episodes” is capitalized in “four *Episodes*” but lowercased in the preceding callout.
  Choose whether it is a formal local structural term.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The debugging/architecture analogy converts a potentially dutiful history chapter into
  a story with stakes and a recognizable plot.

#### Episode I heading (lines 27–28)

- **Function:** The H1 exception is deliberate and documented in `STYLE.md`; the title is active and economical.
- **Accessibility:** A screen-reader/table-of-contents consumer may interpret four additional H1s as four separate
  documents. The visual hierarchy is defensible, but H2 episode headings with H3 numbered sections would be more
  semantically conventional; any change must be weighed against the explicitly adjudicated local style.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** “The Classical Model Breaks” supplies immediate narrative tension.

#### §0.1 opening and five classical assumptions (lines 29–43)

- **Pedagogy:** Naming the assumptions before breaking them is excellent advance organization. It gives readers a
  retrieval frame for a name-heavy chapter.
- **Major historical/conceptual suspicion:** “Separability—the joint state ... is fully specified by the states
  of its parts” is not a generic classical assumption without qualification. Classical correlated joint
  probability distributions are not fixed by their marginals either. What classical theories typically allow is
  a complete joint state assembled from definite subsystem variables plus correlations; entanglement defeats
  separability in a more specific sense. Current wording risks teaching an overstrong classical/quantum contrast.
- **Precision suspicion:** “Commutativity—the order in which two measurements ... does not affect the results”
  is also too universal as classical practice: invasive classical measurements can disturb systems and order can
  matter operationally. The intended contrast is between ideal observables/functions on classical state space
  and noncommuting quantum observables. Add “in the idealized classical state model.”
- **Continuity qualification:** Classical statistical mechanics already has discrete models, and classical
  particles can occupy bound regimes. Frame continuity as the relevant default for energy exchange/dynamical
  variables, not an axiom of all classical models.
- **Cross-reference defect:** “Each is overturned in §0.2 or §0.4” cannot be literally right for separability,
  whose natural payoff is entanglement in §0.6. Measurement back-action is also formalized later than §0.4.
  Replace with “across §§0.2–0.6” or give per-item destinations.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The checklist makes the reader anticipate controlled demolition.

#### §0.1 Newtonian mechanics paragraph

- **Precision:** “Given the state ... fixed it at every later time” assumes existence/uniqueness and ignores
  singularities or pathological force laws. For this historical level, “under the well-posed laws used in its
  successful regime” would close the philosophical loophole without derailing the prose.
- **Historical calibration:** “No obvious failures in its native regime” is appropriately bounded and avoids
  claiming universal success.
- **Rhythm:** The paragraph repeats the determinism bullet almost verbatim. Use the space to distinguish phase
  space from trajectories, or shorten it.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Clear but mainly expository.

#### §0.1 Maxwell paragraph

- **Correctness:** The broad history and dates are plausible and appropriately high-level.
- **Slight doubt:** “By the 1890s, electromagnetism had absorbed optics” is rhetorically strong; geometrical
  optics and material response still involved approximations and constitutive models. “Unified the central wave
  phenomena of optics” is safer.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The sequence from unification to Hertz to apparent closure moves briskly.

#### §0.1 thermodynamics/statistical-mechanics paragraph

- **Positive:** The fine-grained/coarse-grained distinction is unusually responsible for an introductory history
  and heads off the simplistic “irreversibility from reversible laws” claim.
- **Precision suspicion:** Coarse-grained spreading is one explanatory framework; typicality, boundary conditions,
  molecular chaos, and low-entropy initial conditions carry additional load. Calling it “the ... origin” is too
  singular. Prefer “a central part of the statistical-mechanical account.”
- **Selective-reader load:** Liouville's theorem, phase-space volume, and macrostates arrive without definitions.
  The paragraph remains intelligible, but one appositive defining phase space would fulfill the book's cold-entry
  principle.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Intellectually satisfying, though denser than its neighbors.

#### §0.1 confidence, Kelvin, and “two clouds” paragraph

- **Major source/historical caution:** The “respectable physicist in 1894” is an unattributed allusion—likely
  Michelson or a commonly circulated paraphrase—and Kelvin’s “two clouds” story is frequently simplified.
  The prose wisely says “reportedly,” but direct quotation marks around “two small clouds” still imply textual
  fidelity. Attribute the 1894 figure, quote exact sourced wording, or paraphrase both.
- **Precision:** Kelvin's clouds concerned ether-drift and specific-heat/equipartition difficulties; saying the
  latter “became quantum mechanics” is a useful compression, but not the same list as the three anomalies named
  earlier. Signal that this is a related warning sign rather than a one-to-one mapping.
- **Style:** “Both clouds ... contained tornadoes” is vivid and memorable but momentarily shifts into popular-
  science flourish. It earns its place if the book permits one strong metaphor here.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** This is the section’s narrative peak.

#### §0.1 closing synthesis

- **Positive:** The three-interface grouping is a strong compression and cleanly motivates why parameter tuning
  could not suffice.
- **Internal-count defect:** The paragraph says “the four anomalies” after listing black-body radiation,
  photoelectric threshold behavior, and atomic spectra—three explicit anomalies. Atomic stability appears only
  later in the same paragraph, making the count initially look erroneous. Name all four in the first list.
- **Chronology suspicion:** Measurement/state problems are presented as one of the interfaces at which the
  anomalies “sat,” but the empirical/theoretical measurement problem emerged more clearly with the replacement
  formalism than as a late-19th-century anomaly. Separate pre-1900 failures from demands exposed during
  formalization.
- **Overstatement:** “The abstractions themselves ... were wrong” is too blunt because classical abstractions
  remain excellent effective theories in their regimes. “Not fundamental and not valid in the new regime” better
  matches the preceding native-regime qualification.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The ending turns a catalogue into a genuine architectural failure and creates forward
  pull.

#### §0.2 opening sentence

- **Transition:** “The crisis came in pieces” is an efficient handoff from the interface-level synthesis.
- **Overclaim:** Not every piece by itself “falsified a different classical assumption”; spectra exposed missing
  explanatory machinery, while atomic stability depends on the Rutherford/planetary model and radiation theory.
  “Exposed a distinct failure” is epistemically safer than “falsified.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.2 black-body radiation

- **Pedagogy:** The chain mode density → equipartition → divergent integral → contradiction is compact and
  genuinely explanatory.
- **Technical precision:** The text says equipartition gives `½ k_B T` per quadratic degree and therefore
  `k_B T` per cavity mode “since a mode has two.” That is broadly right for the oscillator's two quadratic terms,
  but readers may confuse this with two photon polarizations, which separately affect density of states. Say
  “two quadratic oscillator terms” explicitly.
- **Notation/style:** The Prelude's Unicode-in-code convention is intentional, but long inline expressions such as
  spectral laws are visually less legible than the narrative around them. Consider display-style indented text
  or a small comparison table if renderer robustness permits.
- **Historical nuance:** Rayleigh–Jeans was not simply a single “classical derivation” fully formed at the first
  crisis; the historical sequencing of Rayleigh (1900) and Jeans (1905) matters if dates elsewhere invite a strict
  chronology. A small date label would help.
- **Rhetoric:** “Wrong by infinity” is superb and mathematically meaningful, not empty drama.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.2 atomic spectra

- **Potential formula defect:** “frequencies are proportional to `1/n² − 1/m²` for small positive integers
  `n,m`” omits ordering and the fixed Balmer lower level. Depending on convention, the expression may be negative.
  State `m>n` and that Balmer fixes `n=2` (or give the wavelength/wavenumber form).
- **Historical precision:** The Rydberg formula generalized hydrogen series, not “across ... into other elements”
  in the same exact one-electron form. Alkali/other spectra require modifications. Narrow the claim.
- **Pedagogy:** The line “no ... meaning” makes the integers feel like a mystery worth solving.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.2 photoelectric effect

- **Positive:** Threshold, intensity, and kinetic-energy dependencies are separated cleanly.
- **Nuance:** Classical wave theory's predicted time delay/energy accumulation is another diagnostic and would
  strengthen the contrast, but adding it may overfill this compact catalogue.
- **Precision suspicion:** “Classical electromagnetism predicted the opposite” is a useful shorthand but depends
  on a classical model of electron binding/energy transfer, not Maxwell's equations alone. Say “the classical
  wave-plus-electron model.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.2 atomic stability

- **Major historical nuance:** Rutherford established nuclear scattering/structure; “orbited by electrons” is the
  subsequent planetary interpretation, not a direct gold-foil observation. Rephrase accordingly.
- **Fact-check need:** The `10⁻¹¹ s` collapse time is order-of-magnitude and model-dependent. Source it or say
  “on an atomic timescale (often estimated around...).”
- **Rhetoric:** “Atoms should not exist. They do.” is maximally efficient and memorable.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.2 synthesis

- **Precision:** The conclusion restores the four-item count and is much clearer than §0.1's delayed fourth item.
- **Residual overreach:** “failed exactly where matter, radiation, and measurement touched” includes measurement,
  but none of §0.2's four items centrally tests measurement back-action. “Matter and radiation touched the
  microscopic world; measurement would become part of the replacement” would preserve the arc.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.3 opening

- **Positive:** “The first response was to patch” sustains the developer metaphor without explaining it again.
- **Nuance:** “They never quite did” is fair retrospectively; avoid implying contemporaries shared a single
  explicit software-like integration program.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.3 Planck paragraph

- **Technical strength:** Both low- and high-frequency limits are given, so the reader sees *how* the fix repairs
  the catastrophe rather than merely hearing that it does.
- **Historical suspicion:** The account of Planck's interpretive resistance is a contested historiographic area;
  the exact “purely formal” quote and “fifteen years trying to wish it away” need especially careful sourcing.
  The latter is colorful mind-reading. Replace with documented attempts to retain classical interpretation.
- **Potential factual defect:** “The formula matched experiment with no free parameters” is too strong: temperature,
  normalization/constants, and empirical determination of $h$ enter, even if no arbitrary spectral-fit shape
  parameters remain. Specify what “no free parameters” means.
- **Potential symbol ambiguity:** Saying $h$ joined `$c$ and $e$` can make `$e$` look like Euler's number rather
  than elementary charge. Spell out elementary charge.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The reluctant-discoverer story and asymptotic limits make this unusually alive.

#### §0.3 Einstein paragraph

- **Correctness:** The work-function explanation is clean and locally self-contained.
- **Historical precision:** Einstein's 1905 light-quantum paper was not simply “taking Planck's discreteness”
  without modification; Planck quantized oscillator energy exchanges, while Einstein made the radical light-
  quantum move. The paragraph mostly conveys this, but sharpen the distinction.
- **Nobel phrasing suspicion:** “the only one ... considered settled enough” compresses committee politics and
  the citation wording into inferred motivation. Quote or paraphrase the actual citation and avoid asserting
  committee psychology without source.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.3 Rutherford paragraph

- **Historical defect risk:** A uniform Thomson charge distribution did not provide a generally accepted “stable
  answer”; the plum-pudding model had its own dynamical/spectral difficulties. “No immediate radiative-collapse
  picture of an orbiting point charge” is safer.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §0.3 Bohr paragraph

- **Positive:** Success and inconsistency are held together honestly; the reader sees why the theory mattered and
  why it could not be final.
- **Terminology:** Bohr's original angular-momentum condition is better written $L=n\hbar`; “integer values ...
  in units” is fine but less exact.
- **Historical nuance:** “Bohr was the first to articulate the inconsistency as a feature” is a priority claim
  needing a source or softer wording.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.3 Sommerfeld and Stern–Gerlach

- **Positive:** The retrospective spin correction is exactly the kind of historical caveat popular summaries omit.
- **Precision suspicion:** Sommerfeld's relativistic fine-structure success contains the famous accidental
  agreement and does not capture modern spin/QED corrections. “Obtained much of the observed fine structure
  within the model” would avoid over-crediting.
- **Stern–Gerlach nuance:** Silver's splitting reflects total atomic magnetic moment dominated by the unpaired
  electron; calling it simply the electron's spin is acceptable later interpretation but deserves that nuance.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.3 Compton, de Broglie, Pauli sequence

- **Local defect:** “particle-like behaviour of light ... in the same experiment that had previously been read as
  wave-like” is unclear—Compton scattering was not a single earlier wave-interpreted experiment in the way this
  wording suggests. Say it exposed particle-like exchange in radiation already known to diffract/interfere.
- **Chronology:** Davisson–Germer (1927) is deliberately forward evidence inside a 1900–1925 section. Flag it as
  later confirmation, which the parenthetical date partly does.
- **Pauli precision:** The exclusion principle did not originally introduce the modern spin degree of freedom in
  quite the clean way stated; Pauli's two-valuedness and the later Uhlenbeck–Goudsmit spin interpretation should
  be distinguished.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The rapid reciprocal wave/particle progression has strong momentum.

#### §0.3 closing assessment

- **Positive:** The success/failure parallel is excellent and the list of missing intensities, selection rules,
  multi-electron atoms, and molecules concretely justifies replacement.
- **Historical suspicion:** Credit for periodic-table shell structure by 1925 was partial and theory-laden; qualify
  “working formulas” versus qualitative organization.
- **Conceptual precision:** The list says old theory quantized “spin projections,” but spin was only introduced in
  1925 and not yet integrated into old quantum theory. “space-quantized angular-momentum projections” is more
  historically exact.
- **Style:** “Conceptual budget,” “patched,” and “complete rewrite” form a coherent metaphor cluster; it is close
  to saturation but still controlled.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The final antithesis—too useful to ignore, too inconsistent to trust—is an excellent
  episode cliffhanger.

#### Episode II heading

- **Function:** Clear escalation from broken model to replacement formalism.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.4 reader note and survival definitions

- **Positive:** Explicitly telling readers what *not* to retain is excellent cognitive-load management.
- **Conceptual concern:** “Different bases correspond to different measurement choices” is true for orthonormal
  projective bases but not for general POVMs; add “in the projective cases discussed here.”
- **Precision:** “Eigenvalues ... are the only possible outcomes” likewise assumes an ideal projective measurement
  of that observable.
- **Style:** Five definitions before the history help cold entry but interrupt narrative momentum. A shaded
  “survival kit” is exactly the right visual form if rendering supports it.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.4 Heisenberg matrix mechanics

- **Historical caution:** The Helgoland/hay-fever anecdote is engaging but should not crowd out the published
  paper's actual conceptual route; ensure the source notes support it.
- **Technical defect:** The canonical commutation relation cannot hold for finite-dimensional matrices (trace of a
  commutator is zero). Since the prose has just defined matrix mechanics as matrices and the book targets qubits,
  note that $x,p$ are unbounded operators in an infinite-dimensional space.
- **Overclaim:** Not “every subsequent restriction” follows from this one relation without domain/state
  conditions. Say it generates the standard position–momentum uncertainty structure.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.4 Schrödinger wave mechanics

- **Precision:** The displayed Hamiltonian writes $V(x)$ but immediately applies a radial $V(r)$; harmless, yet
  $V(\mathbf r)$ would align dimensions.
- **Historical/math nuance:** “normalisable solutions only for discrete energies” omits hydrogen's positive-energy
  continuum scattering states, which are delta-normalized rather than square-normalizable. Say the *bound-state*
  solutions have the discrete spectrum.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.4 equivalence

- **Positive:** The API metaphor is well chosen and bounded by “preserves every observable prediction.”
- **Precision:** “Matrix mechanics is wave mechanics expressed in the energy basis” is too narrow; matrix
  representations can use any basis. Say each is a representation of the same abstract operator theory.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.4 Born interpretation and classical-probability contrast

- **Conceptual overstatement:** Classical probabilities need not always be epistemic; objective-chance,
  stochastic-process, and propensity interpretations exist. State that *ordinary hidden-state engineering
  models* often use epistemic uncertainty.
- **Positive:** The following paragraph carefully restores the operational/interpretational boundary and local
  qualifier; this partially repairs the initial contrast.
- **Historical suspicion:** Explaining the 1954 Nobel delay as reflecting slow absorption is plausible but causal
  speculation. Source it or avoid the inference.
- **Technical strength:** Phase/interference is connected directly to computation without pretending amplitudes
  are readable data.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.4 two-slit explanation

- **Positive:** Excellent local explanation of adding amplitudes before squaring.
- **Physical precision:** Single-slit patterns are diffraction envelopes, not literally “single bright stripes.”
  This simplification can mislead when the two-slit pattern is the product of envelope and interference fringes.
- **Interpretational caution:** “amplitude from the left/right slit” is path-decomposition language, not a claim
  that the particle took a definite path; one phrase could prevent that reading.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.4 uncertainty

- **Positive:** It distinguishes Heisenberg's heuristic argument, Kennard's variance bound, and Robertson's
  generalization—excellent historical precision.
- **Mathematical qualification:** Robertson's bound requires suitable domain conditions for unbounded operators;
  unnecessary to derive here but “for states/operators for which these quantities are defined” would be exact.
- **Potential overstatement:** Nonzero commutator does not universally mean two observables cannot share *any*
  common eigenstate; the state-dependent expectation of the commutator can vanish. The prose's categorical final
  sentence about spin components is fine, but generalize carefully elsewhere.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.4 uncertainty versus disturbance

- **Positive:** This is a crucial and unusually explicit distinction.
- **Precision concern:** “measuring ... in a basis it is not already prepared in ... projecting it” assumes ideal
  projective measurement; later POVM/channel caveats should be foreshadowed.
- **Wording:** “wrong basis” is pedagogically dangerous—there is no intrinsically wrong basis, only one
  incompatible with/preparation-disturbing relative to the task.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.4 Bohr and conclusion

- **Historical/interpretive caution:** Complementarity cannot be reduced merely to apparatus selecting a
  description without losing Bohr's subtleties; label this as the operational takeaway.
- **Overclaim:** “By 1927 the mathematical core was settled” overlooks later Hilbert-space formalization, density
  operators, POVMs, quantum field theory, and foundational disputes. “The calculational core” is safer.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.5 Dirac

- **Positive:** The 1939 bra–ket dating correction is exactly the precision a historical chapter needs.
- **Overstatement:** The Dirac equation's negative-energy structure “predicted antimatter” is standard shorthand,
  but Dirac's interpretation evolved; note prediction of positron-like solutions and later hole interpretation.
- **Style:** The antimatter detour is fascinating but only loosely tied to quantum computing. It earns its space
  as evidence of framework power, though one sentence could be cut.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.5 von Neumann — Hilbert-space survival definition

- **Positive:** Finite-dimensional specialization is carefully and usefully explained.
- **Precision:** “limits of sequences inside it stay inside it” needs “Cauchy sequences”; arbitrary sequence limits
  need not exist.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §0.5 von Neumann — correspondence catalogue

- **Conceptual precision:** Pure physical states are rays, not unique unit vectors; global phase equivalence should
  be acknowledged even in a historical summary.
- **Potential attribution issue:** The long list risks attributing all modern textbook correspondences in their
  final form solely to von Neumann. Distinguish codification from sole invention.
- **Long-sentence issue:** The sentence containing states, observables, measurement, composites, exponential
  dimension, Feynman, computational power, and density operators is overloaded. Split it; this is a reference
  hinge, not a place to optimize for compression.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §0.5 distinguishability, POVM caveat, and developer interface

- **Technical concern:** “A genuinely pure state and genuinely mixed state are operationally distinguishable with
  enough copies” needs conditions: if the candidate states are known/distinct, asymptotic discrimination is
  possible, but no finite universal measurement certifies “pure versus mixed” for an otherwise unknown arbitrary
  state without tomography/assumptions.
- **Positive:** Ensemble decompositions versus density operators are distinguished correctly and importantly.
- **Terminology:** Calling measurement “projection” in the developer recap immediately after introducing POVMs
  narrows the interface again. Use “measurement operations (projective in the first model).”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.6 opening and Solvay

- **Positive:** Problem → resource is a powerful organizing arc.
- **Historical caution:** The Solvay paragraph compresses Einstein's evolving objections and Bohr's responses into
  a hidden-variable/completeness debate that crystallized later. Avoid presenting 1927 positions entirely in
  later Bell-era vocabulary.
- **Nobel repetition:** The Einstein Nobel fact has already appeared in §0.3; repetition is justified for cold
  entry but linear readers may feel it.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.6 EPR

- **Precision:** The original EPR criterion of reality and assumptions deserve explicit naming; the summary moves
  too quickly from predictable alternatives to simultaneous definite values.
- **Major overstatement:** “correlations ... that have no analogue in any classical joint distribution” is false
  for perfect same-basis correlations alone. The nonclassicality appears across incompatible settings/Bell
  inequalities. The later Bell-state paragraph correctly says this; fix the earlier sentence.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.6 Bohm bridge

- **Positive:** Including Bohm as both an existence proof for hidden variables and a demonstration of nonlocal
  cost is excellent.
- **Precision:** “Reproduces every prediction” should be scoped to nonrelativistic quantum mechanics under quantum
  equilibrium; extensions exist but the unqualified universal is too broad.
- **Style:** “Blatantly non-local” has argumentative heat. “Explicitly nonlocal” is more neutral.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.6 Schrödinger and pre-Bell interval

- **Potential misstatement:** Entangling a macroscopic object with a microscopic one produces a correlated joint
  state; calling “the resulting superposition” devoid of classical description skips decoherence/mixed local
  states. Fine historically, but tighten.
- **Overstatement:** “No experiment could distinguish” should be “no known inequality/experimental proposal then
  separated the predictions under stated assumptions.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.6 Bell and CHSH

- **Positive:** The one-line CHSH derivation is superb and makes the classical bound feel inevitable.
- **Assumption precision:** Predetermined values for all four settings combine locality with realism/counterfactual
  definiteness or follow through a deterministic completion; stochastic local models can be reduced but that step
  is unstated.
- **Tsirelson dating:** If “Tsirelson bound, 1980” is a priority/date claim, verify transliteration and publication
  year consistently with sources.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.6 Aspect

- **Major historical defect:** Calling Aspect's work “the first experiments specifically designed to close the
  locality loophole” is likely too strong, and “ensured that no signal ... could ... affect” overstates the
  periodic/deterministic switching and separation limitations. The text later qualifies “within limits,” but the
  stronger sentence should be softened.
- **Fact-check consistency:** This topic already generated stale anchors elsewhere; unify the exact approved
  historical wording across Chapters 3 and the Prelude.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.6 detection and freedom-of-choice loopholes

- **Positive:** Threshold distinction between maximally and non-maximally entangled states is excellent.
- **Precision suspicion:** Threshold efficiencies depend on assumptions, background counts, and inequality; label
  the quoted values as idealized canonical thresholds.
- **Terminology:** Experiments do not close freedom of choice “to a strong degree” in a metric directly comparable
  with locality/detection. The paragraph itself understands this; use “constrain conventional common-cause
  windows.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.6 2015 loophole-free tests

- **Statistical wording defect:** A p-value below $10^{-6}$ is not “the chance that a locally classical world
  would by accident produce” the observed result; it is a tail probability under a specified null/model and
  analysis protocol. Remove the informal inverse-probability interpretation.
- **Overclaim:** “ruled out ... every local hidden-variable theory” must retain assumptions about setting
  independence, memory, analysis, and experimental model. The prior paragraph knows this; repeat the qualifier in
  the conclusion.
- **Positive:** Differentiating Delft's historic priority from photonic statistical strength is valuable.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.6 Bell-state concrete example

- **Positive:** It explicitly states that same-basis correlation alone is not the Bell violation; suitable settings
  are required. This correction should be propagated backward to the EPR paragraph.
- **Precision:** “cannot be reduced to independent states” describes non-product states but classical correlated
  mixtures are also non-product. Define entanglement as nonseparability—not expressible as a convex mixture of
  product states—for mixed-state generality, or say this pure state cannot factor.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.6 protocols: dense coding and teleportation

- **Resource-accounting nuance:** Dense coding's advantage assumes a pre-shared ebit; “no purely classical
  protocol can match” needs equal accounting of prior shared resources/channel dimensions.
- **Teleportation wording:** The “quantum step is instantaneous in the formal description” invites exactly the
  collapse/signaling misconception the paragraph fights. Say Bob's conditional state relation is established
  without an operationally detectable instantaneous change.
- **Error-correction link:** Calling teleportation *the* template error correction later reuses is partly true for
  teleportation-based/gate-teleportation schemes, not all QEC. Qualify.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.6 E91 and closing arc

- **Major factual defect:** The section lists “superdense coding, teleportation, **BB84**” as the arc of
  entanglement becoming operational. Standard BB84 does not require entanglement (though an equivalent
  entanglement-based formulation exists). Replace BB84 with E91 or explicitly name the entanglement-based
  reformulation.
- **Security precision:** A Bell violation alone is not universally sufficient to certify “no eavesdropper”; E91
  security depends on protocol assumptions and observed error/correlation bounds. “Bounds Eve's information”
  is safer.
- **Positive:** The final problem → name → test → resource compression is memorable and structurally clean.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### Episode III heading and bridge

- **Positive:** The explicit bridge prevents the history from feeling like a “great physicists” sequence detached
  from computing.
- **Minor rhetoric:** “Built the world the experienced developer already lives in” is strong and largely earned,
  though software developers also inhabit technologies whose relevant operation is well modeled classically.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.7 opening map of engineering revolutions

- **Internal-count defect:** The prose announces “four quiet revolutions,” lists semiconductor, laser, NMR, and
  superconductivity, but the section later calls them “the same five revolutions” after adding modern materials.
  Decide on four core revolutions plus a broader materials synthesis, or announce five.
- **Factual defect:** Superconductivity did not itself “give us ... medical imaging” in the same direct conceptual
  way NMR did; superconducting magnets enabled modern MRI hardware. Separate the measurement principle (NMR) from
  magnet engineering.
- **Precision:** “Single spin populations” is awkward for ensemble NMR; early NMR quantum computing manipulated
  ensemble-averaged nuclear-spin states, not individually addressable single spins.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.7 semiconductor physics and transistor

- **Technical overcompression:** Conductors/insulators/semiconductors are not classified merely by whether a band
  gap is “small enough to bridge thermally or electrically”; Fermi level, doping, mobility, temperature, and band
  filling matter. Keep the intuition but label it simplified.
- **Historical precision:** The first transistor used germanium, not silicon; the paragraph mentions both later,
  but “doped silicon and germanium” could be reordered to avoid implying silicon in the 1947 device.
- **Causal overreach:** GPUs and memory sit downstream of much more than band theory. “Depend on semiconductor
  band engineering” is precise and sufficient.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.7 stimulated emission, masers, and lasers

- **Potential historical defect:** Einstein's A/B coefficient derivation used quantum transitions but also a
  semiclassical equilibrium argument; “no classical counterpart” is reasonable for stimulated emission yet
  rhetorically absolute.
- **Grammar:** “The maser (Townes, Basov, Prokhorov, 1953–1954)” conflates independent/contemporaneous theoretical
  and experimental contributions. Separate Townes/Gordon/Zeiger's first ammonia maser from Basov–Prokhorov's work.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.7 NMR

- **Historical defect:** Rabi's molecular-beam magnetic resonance work predates bulk NMR, which Bloch and Purcell
  independently demonstrated in 1945–46. “Developed in the 1940s” is fine, but grouping all three under NMR
  obscures the lineage.
- **Overstatement:** “Every quantum-mechanical technique short of full computation” is plainly too broad. NMR did
  not rehearse single-photon optics, projective single-shot readout, non-Abelian anyons, and much else.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.7 superconductivity

- **Factual defect:** MRI is already credited to NMR and reappears here under superconductivity; the conceptual and
  enabling-hardware roles need disambiguation.
- **Technical nuance:** “Single phase” is an introductory shorthand for macroscopic phase coherence; finite
  systems support spatial phase variation, vortices, and broken-symmetry subtleties.
- **Platform accuracy:** Transmons use Josephson junction circuits; they do not simply “sit on top of BCS theory.”
  The chain is BCS/macroscopic superconductivity → Josephson effect → nonlinear circuit.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.7 modern materials paragraph and five-row table

- **Overbreadth:** “Accounts for” every listed macroscopic property is too totalizing; condensed-matter theory
  explains/model-predicts them at varying fidelity.
- **Table defect:** “Condensed-matter/materials” row includes **ultracold gases**, which are atomic physics rather
  than condensed-matter materials, even if they simulate condensed-matter Hamiltonians.
- **Table defect:** Superconducting control is generally millikelvin, not “sub-millikelvin”; dilution refrigerators
  operate around 10–20 mK. Fix this likely unit/order-of-magnitude error.
- **Table suspicion:** Dispersive cavity readout uses microwave sources/amplifiers; classifying it as a consequence
  of the laser/maser revolution is arguable but should say microwave photonics rather than “laser.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The inheritance table is a useful compression despite taxonomy issues.

#### §0.7 asymmetry and first/second quantum revolutions

- **Positive:** The substrate-versus-information distinction is load-bearing and very well stated.
- **Conceptual nuance:** Classical computers also encode information in physical quantum-derived states; the
  distinctive feature is controlled coherence/entanglement across computational degrees of freedom, not merely
  “use quantum state directly.”
- **Attribution suspicion:** “The phrase is due to (among others) Alain Aspect” is vague. Either give a traceable
  source/history or omit the priority aside.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.8 Turing

- **Precision:** Turing's universal machine and computability analysis are distinct contributions; the 1936 paper
  defines machines and proves universality/undecidability in context. Current compression is acceptable.
- **Terminology:** “Right input” could be “encoded description and input,” which makes universality concrete.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.8 Church–Turing thesis

- **Major conceptual defect:** The Church–Turing thesis is not merely an “empirical observation” and “no proposal
  has yet broken” it; it identifies effectively calculable functions with Turing-computable functions and is
  supported by convergence of formalizations. Physical hypercomputation proposals do not straightforwardly
  “break” the mathematical thesis.
- **Complexity nuance:** The extended/strong thesis has multiple formulations. Quantum computing challenges
  efficient **classical probabilistic simulation**, not necessarily every version stated with physical models.
- **Unsupported implication:** “Classical machines with randomness are strictly more practical” is not a formal
  reason BPP is the comparison, and whether BPP strictly exceeds P is unknown. Say randomness is an allowed
  classical resource and BPP is the robust bounded-error baseline.
- **BQP history:** Saying Bernstein–Vazirani “define properly” BQP needs historical checking; the class/name lineage
  includes Deutsch/Jozsa and Bernstein–Vazirani complexity formalization.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.8 early digital computers

- **Factual defect:** “By 1970 it was a consumer product in slow motion” is colorful but obscure and arguably
  premature; consumer microcomputers arrived mainly in the mid/late 1970s. Say the enabling industrial trajectory
  was established.
- **List precision:** EDVAC and EDSAC dates/construction/operation differ; use first-operation dates consistently.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.8 concluding role

- **Positive:** The abstraction → physical-instantiation question is a crisp reason this otherwise short detour
  belongs.
- **Minor repetition:** The final italic sentence restates the preceding sentence almost exactly. Keep the sharper
  italic version and trim the setup.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.9 Shannon

- **Major technical defect:** Shannon entropy is not generally “the average length of the shortest binary encoding”
  for single symbols. It lower-bounds expected prefix-code length (within less than one bit) and is the asymptotic
  optimal rate per symbol for long iid blocks under source coding.
- **Channel nuance:** Capacity is a maximized reliable asymptotic rate under a specified channel/input model, not
  simply a property explained without assumptions.
- **Positive:** Bits as operational currency and forward link to quantum entropy are effective.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.9 Landauer

- **Technical concern:** The “known bit can be erased reversibly by an undo step paid elsewhere” explanation is
  potentially confusing. Landauer cost depends on entropy reduction and side information/physical protocol; a
  logically irreversible map can have no work cost on a known input distribution while resetting the correlated
  memory that supplied knowledge may move the cost.
- **Precision:** The lower bound is $k_B T\ln 2$ under ideal quasistatic conditions; use $k_B`, not bare $k`, for
  unambiguous notation.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.9 Maxwell's demon

- **Historical/conceptual overstatement:** Bennett's memory-erasure account is central, but modern analyses of
  measurement, feedback, correlations, and generalized thermodynamics are subtler than “exactly enough” per
  recorded molecule. Avoid presenting one toy bookkeeping story as the universally complete exorcism.
- **Positive:** “The demon does not pay for measuring; it pays for forgetting” is memorable, provided it is
  qualified as the idealized reversible-measurement analysis.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.9 Bennett reversible computation

- **Major technical defect:** The AND example maps **two input bits to one output bit**, so more than “the discarded
  bit” is involved and the information loss is distribution-dependent; three inputs mapping to zero cannot be
  inverted with just one binary history bit in the naive representation.
- **Thermodynamic overstatement:** Logically reversible computation has no *Landauer lower bound from logical
  erasure*, not “no thermodynamic minimum cost at all.” Finite-time operation, noise, control, initialization, and
  reliability can impose dissipation.
- **Uncomputation description:** Uncomputation does not generally leave an arbitrary result untouched unless the
  result has been coherently copied into a suitable register (typically via CNOT for computational-basis data) or
  phase-kickback structure is used. State the compute–copy/use–uncompute pattern.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.9 synthesis and italic thesis

- **Positive:** The convergence of information theory and physics is stated with exceptional clarity.
- **Philosophical caution:** “Almost forced” and “deepest model ... cannot remain classical” are rhetorical, not a
  logical deduction from Landauer. Label the italic statement as Wheeler-style motivation rather than theorem.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.10 Wiesner

- **Historical suspicion:** Claims that the paper united “no-cloning” before the theorem and “oblivious transfer”
  need careful retrospective wording. The mechanisms anticipate these ideas; they were not necessarily formulated
  under later definitions/security models.
- **Security precision:** Bank verification and unforgeability depend on secret classical descriptions, ideal
  states, and attack model; nonorthogonality plus no-cloning alone is not a complete security proof.
- **Mind-reading:** “journals had no community sharp enough” is entertaining but unfair/speculative. Say there was
  no established venue/community.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.10 no-cloning

- **Positive:** Correctly limits the theorem to arbitrary unknown states and unitary copying with a blank ancilla.
- **Nuance:** Orthogonal known-set states can be copied, and approximate/probabilistic cloning exists. One brief
  caveat would prevent readers from overgeneralizing the memorable prohibition.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.10 BB84 mechanism

- **Security precision:** QKD security is not supplied by no-cloning and disturbance alone; authenticated classical
  communication, error correction, privacy amplification, device assumptions, and quantitative proofs are
  necessary. Call those the physical primitives, not the complete security guarantee.
- **Wording:** Again replace “wrong/mismatched basis” where it might imply an objectively incorrect measurement;
  “basis different from preparation” is neutral.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.10 BB84 deployment history

- **Potential factual defect:** “Twin-field protocols ... stretched achievable distance into thousands of
  kilometres” needs exact distinction between deployed fiber, laboratory spools, trusted-node networks, and
  satellite links. Twin-field QKD fiber demonstrations and Micius satellite distances should not be merged into a
  single achievement.
- **Overstatement:** “Deployable engineering discipline” is defensible in niches, but QKD's practical security
  record and trusted-device/network limitations deserve a hint even in the prelude.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.10 QKD versus PQC

- **Positive:** This distinction is essential, current, and well explained.
- **Perishability:** Standards names/status are date-sensitive and should be linked to the moving-target/fact-check
  ledger rather than embedded here without a dated warning.
- **Precision:** QKD does still require computational/authentication assumptions or an initially shared secret for
  classical-channel authentication; “security resting on laws ... rather than computational assumption” is too
  absolute.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.10 uncertainty versus disturbance reprise

- **Positive:** Repetition is pedagogically justified because the confusion is common.
- **Technical issue:** An intercept-resend eavesdropper causes detectable disturbance statistically; general
  eavesdroppers are handled by information–disturbance/security proofs, not simply “cannot avoid disturbing.”
- **Irreversibility:** Projective measurement update is not universally physically irreversible at the global
  system–apparatus level; it is operationally irreversible when the record/environment is discarded. The current
  categorical wording conflicts with the later unitary-environment story.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.10 Episode III conclusion

- **Positive:** The two independent transitions and their convergence are a strong macro-structure.
- **Chronology wording:** “By the time anyone proposed quantum computation” is too broad given Benioff's 1980
  proposal and continued maturation of quantum-information protocols afterward. Say “By the time quantum
  computation became a sustained research program.”
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### Episode IV heading

- **Positive:** “Becomes a discipline” accurately promises institutions, algorithms, architecture, and engineering
  rather than another isolated discovery.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.11 opening and Benioff

- **Chronology defect:** “Three steps, spread over five years” names Benioff/Manin 1980, Feynman 1981/82, and
  Deutsch 1985, but the section later adds circuit equivalence through 1993 and Lloyd 1996. Scope the “three
  founding steps” explicitly.
- **Precision:** Benioff did more than prove “no in-principle obstruction”; he supplied a Hamiltonian model of
  computation. The following sentence makes this clear.
- **Causal nuance:** Reversibility makes a unitary embedding possible, but Benioff's construction and Bennett's
  reversible simulation have distinct histories; “rests on” may overstate direct dependence.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.11 Manin

- **Historical caution:** Priority language (“genuine independent origins”) and circulation claims need a robust
  source, ideally including the exact translated passage.
- **Technical concern:** A generic many-particle system does not necessarily require $2^n$ parameters unless the
  components are two-level and arbitrary pure states are considered. State that canonical qubit specialization.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.11 Feynman

- **Major conceptual correction:** Nature does not literally “get [the statevector description] for free”; this
  anthropomorphic storage metaphor can imply $2^n$ locally accessible physical resources. Say quantum dynamics
  realizes the evolution without explicitly representing every amplitude classically.
- **Overstatement:** “No known classical algorithm ... for generic interacting systems” needs the target task,
  accuracy, time, and representation specified; classical algorithms can sample/approximate restricted observables.
- **Circular phrasing:** Calling simulation “intractable” before establishing a complexity assumption sounds
  proven. Use “appears exponentially costly in the general case.”
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.11 Deutsch

- **Historical precision:** Verify the exact success/inconclusive probabilities and comparison model for Deutsch's
  original algorithm; the modern deterministic problem and original formulation differ in promise/output.
- **Major conceptual defect:** One quantum query versus two **deterministic** classical queries does not establish
  lower query complexity against bounded-error randomized classical computation in a straightforward way.
  Preserve the comparison qualifier in the concluding claim.
- **Overstatement:** “Proved it can simulate any physical system that obeys quantum mechanics” needs an efficient-
  simulation/universality formulation and assumptions; universal QTMs simulate quantum computations, not every
  continuous physical system without encoding/approximation qualifications.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.11 organizing sentence

- **Rhetoric:** Possible/necessary/universal is memorable but “necessary” is philosophical; quantum hardware is
  not proved necessary for every useful quantum simulation. “Motivated by apparent classical simulation cost”
  is exact.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.11 circuits and Yao

- **Positive:** Explains why historical machine models disappear from the rest of the book.
- **Technical qualification:** Polynomial equivalence depends on uniformity, approximation, gate sets, and
  computational model. Add “under the standard bounded-error/uniform definitions.”
- **Analogy suspicion:** A circuit model is closer to a gate-level IR than an instruction set; quantum gates may
  compile far below/above native operations. The ISA analogy remains useful if labeled approximate.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.11 Lloyd

- **Overstatement:** “Any local quantum system” and “essentially every physically realistic many-body system”
  omit unbounded local Hilbert spaces, long-range interactions, time dependence, singular terms, state preparation,
  and observable extraction. Say finite-dimensional/local Hamiltonians satisfying stated norm/access conditions.
- **Complexity precision:** Early product-formula dependence on inverse error and term norms should not be
  summarized as generically efficient without encoding/input assumptions.
- **Anachronism:** Calling the 1996 model “exactly” Deutsch's is too strong; the standard gate model developed
  across multiple papers.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.12 anti-hype checkpoint

- **Positive:** One of the manuscript's clearest and most valuable callouts.
- **Precision:** “Superposition ... is free” is rhetorically useful but physically false—state preparation can be
  expensive, and uniform Hadamard superposition is cheap only under a gate model. Replace with “uniform
  superposition can be cheap.”
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.12 oracle/query definitions

- **Major conceptual defect:** An exponential query separation does **not** automatically become a real-world
  speedup when the oracle is efficiently computable. Oracle implementation cost, input construction, promise,
  output, and classical alternatives still matter. Shor is not simply Simon with an oracle replaced by modular
  exponentiation.
- **Positive:** The text otherwise clearly distinguishes diagnostic oracle results from applications.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.12 Deutsch–Jozsa

- **Positive:** Correctly qualifies promise and deterministic comparison.
- **Minor precision:** Randomized classical query complexity under bounded error is constant for distinguishing a
  constant function from a balanced one with high confidence; this makes the “exponential” headline especially
  fragile. State that explicitly, not only “deterministic-rather-than-randomised.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.12 Bernstein–Vazirani

- **Comparison detail:** Classical randomized exact recovery still needs $n$ independent bits/queries; say whether
  the comparison is deterministic/exact.
- **Historical priority:** “First paper to formulate BQP rigorously” needs verification against the exact
  complexity definition and naming history.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.12 Simon

- **Positive:** The bounded-error exponential separation and direct influence on Shor are explained well.
- **Historical anecdote:** STOC rejection/program-committee advocacy is valuable but requires a primary or reliable
  secondary source.
- **Technical precision:** Query lower bound conditions (random oracle satisfying promise/distribution) should be
  named.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.12 Shor overview and order-finding reduction

- **Positive:** The skippable one-paragraph overview is excellent selective-reader design.
- **Technical defect:** The statement that the good conditions occur with probability at least $1/2$ over random
  $a$ is not universal for every composite $N$ as written; standard guarantees require handling even/non-prime-
  power cases and sometimes repeated trials. Add the usual preprocessing/qualified success bound.
- **Notation:** `U_a` is only a permutation/unitary on residues with a carefully defined action over the entire
  computational register; `$|ax\bmod N\rangle$` for out-of-range basis states needs extension.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.12 Shor phase estimation and continued fractions

- **Technical concern:** Starting from a uniform superposition of computational basis states is not the usual
  clean eigenstate account; $|1\rangle$ decomposes over eigenstates of modular multiplication. Explain the actual
  preparation/decomposition to avoid suggesting arbitrary uniform input guarantees phase estimation.
- **Precision:** Continued fractions recover a candidate denominator subject to accuracy/coprimality conditions
  and verification/repetition; “finds” is too deterministic.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.12 Shor resource cost and cryptographic reach

- **Perishable/technical suspicion:** The stated $O(n^2\operatorname{polylog}n)$ gate cost with “Schönhage–
  Strassen-style multiplication” needs a precise circuit model, gate metric, space, and reference. Fast arithmetic
  asymptotics are easy to misstate.
- **Conceptual defect:** Shor discrete log does not work in **arbitrary groups**; efficient quantum algorithms apply
  to suitable finite abelian groups with efficient group operations/encodings.
- **Cryptography nuance:** “Break public-key parts of TLS/SSH/etc.” is better than implying whole protocols become
  automatically broken; authentication, recorded handshakes, forward secrecy, and migration context matter.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.12 symmetric crypto and standards

- **Major oversimplification:** “Restored to safety by doubling key/output sizes” ignores parallelization, circuit
  cost, query access, multi-target attacks, collision algorithms, and different preimage/collision impacts. Use as
  a rule of thumb, not a guarantee.
- **Perishability:** FIPS 206/HQC status is a June/July 2026 moving target and needs a dated warning plus immediate
  re-verification; this book is being reviewed in July 2026.
- **Scope:** Hash outputs do not all merely undergo Grover weakening; collision resistance has different quantum
  query behavior.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.12 Grover

- **Precision:** Classical randomized search lower bound and success-probability assumptions should accompany
  $\Theta(N)$.
- **Amplitude-amplification claim:** “Any quantum subroutine” is too broad; it applies when a success subspace can
  be coherently recognized and the routine/inverse are available.
- **Positive:** Framing Grover as both an advantage and a ceiling is excellent.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.12 Shor/Grover synthesis and harvest-now-decrypt-later

- **Positive:** This is an excellent practical consequences paragraph and distinguishes present safety from
  migration urgency.
- **Precision:** “Shor's exponential speedup” is relative to best known classical algorithms, not a proven
  unconditional lower bound. Repeat “believed.”
- **PQC nuance:** Migration now depends on confidentiality lifetime, migration duration, asset exposure, and threat
  model—not only estimated machine lifetime.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.12 milestone table

- **Potential defect:** Shor's mechanism says QFT “over $\mathbb Z_N$”; order finding uses a QFT over a power-of-two
  control register approximating phases whose period is $r$, not simply the group $\mathbb Z_N$.
- **Consistency:** Simon year appears “1993/1994” while prose calls it Simon (1994); label submission/publication.
- **Taxonomy:** Hamiltonian simulation is not naturally one of “six algorithmic milestones” in the oracle-to-Shor/
  Grover sequence, but inclusion usefully reconnects Feynman.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.12 phase-estimation coda

- **Overstatement:** QPE does not simply “read eigenvalues of any efficiently implementable unitary”; it requires
  controlled powers, suitable eigenstate preparation/overlap, precision resources, and produces classical phase
  estimates probabilistically.
- **HHL/chemistry connection:** Good forward signposting once assumptions are added.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.12 post-1996 widening

- **Major overclaim:** HHL's “exponential-speedup template” is conditional on sparsity, conditioning, state input,
  and state-valued output; flag these here to honor the anti-hype checkpoint.
- **Terminology:** “Qubit-efficient methods” is vague; name the family or remove it.
- **Variational history:** QAOA's manuscript/arXiv/publication year and author naming should be standardized.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.13 decoherence diagnosis

- **Technical defect:** Decoherence does not generally make the reduced state a fully “classical mixture”; it
  suppresses coherences in a system/environment-selected pointer basis to a degree, while residual coherence and
  basis dependence remain.
- **Wording:** “Relative phases ... randomised across an environment” is intuitive but not universally the
  mechanism; environment states become distinguishable and off-diagonal reduced-density terms shrink.
- **Positive:** Global-unitary/local-open distinction is strong.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.13 physical/logical qubits and syndrome

- **Positive:** Essential vocabulary arrives before historical codes.
- **Precision:** Syndromes identify an equivalence class/coset of errors, not always “which error occurred” due to
  degeneracy; correction need only choose an equivalent recovery.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.13 skepticism and measurement/noise distinction

- **Historical caution:** “Dominant view among many physicists” requires evidence beyond two skeptical articles;
  say “a prominent serious objection.”
- **Conceptual issue:** The difference between measurement and noise is not solely intention/recording. Noise may
  be modeled as a channel without literal information leakage, and measurement can be unrecorded; distinguish
  controlled instrument/outcome access from uncontrolled coupling.
- **Overstatement:** Measuring “a copy” does not necessarily collapse the original if independent copies existed;
  the impossibility is creating arbitrary unknown independent copies. Fix this causal explanation.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.13 Shor code formulas

- **Positive:** The normalization $1/(2\sqrt2)$ is correct for three product GHZ factors, and the conceptual
  three-idea preface is helpful.
- **Terminology ambiguity:** “Outer phase-flip code / inner bit-flip code” depends on concatenation convention;
  verify against the exact block ordering shown.
- **Syndrome example suspicion:** `X_1...X_6` stabilizers and indexing need verification; give the complete
  stabilizer set elsewhere and avoid a lone potentially confusing example here.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.13 digitization of continuous errors

- **Precision:** Syndrome measurement projects a coherent error superposition into syndrome sectors, not always a
  single Pauli error—degenerate errors can share a syndrome, and coherent components within a sector can remain.
- **Overclaim:** QEC does not defeat no-cloning and measurement objections through entanglement alone; linear
  encoding, orthogonal syndrome subspaces, Knill–Laflamme conditions, and fresh ancillas do the work.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.13 Steane, CSS, and stabilizers

- **Potential CSS convention defect:** The stated condition and assignment of $C_1`, $C_2`, and $C_2^\perp` vary by
  convention and may be wrong/incomplete; standard CSS requires a dual-containment relation such as
  $C_2^\perp\subseteq C_1$. Verify carefully.
- **Historical defect:** Steane's code is not straightforwardly “the seed” of the CSS family; Calderbank–Shor and
  Steane developed related constructions independently.
- **Overstatement:** Stabilizer formalism does not cover all color/surface variants without qualification and does
  not cover non-stabilizer codes; “most leading qubit codes” is safer.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.13 threshold theorem

- **Technical defect:** Overhead is not simply `poly(log(1/ε)) physical qubits and gates per logical operation` in
  every threshold theorem; it also depends on circuit size, locality, architecture, noise, decoding, and desired
  total failure probability.
- **Technical defect:** Logical-error suppression under concatenation is doubly exponential in concatenation
  level only under recursive effective-noise bounds; say so.
- **Noise assumptions:** The later qualification is good, but “no correlated bursts” is stronger than necessary;
  threshold theorems allow bounded/local correlations under specific norms.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.13 surface code, qLDPC, and conclusion

- **Major current-claim suspicion:** Saying 2024 bivariate-bicycle codes “match [surface-code] threshold while
  substantially improving ... overhead” is architecture/noise/decoder dependent and likely overstates
  demonstrated practicality.
- **Platform defect:** Surface codes map naturally to fixed 2D superconducting layouts; trapped ions often exploit
  nonlocal connectivity and do not map in the same “clean” way.
- **Terminology:** IBM's “gross code” should be explained as a named code instance and capitalized/quoted
  consistently; avoid sounding evaluative.
- **Historical overstatement:** Surface code has not uniformly been the dominant target for trapped-ion platforms.
- **Positive:** The objection → construction → theorem → resource-reduction arc is an excellent close.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §0.14 Cirac–Zoller

- **Overclaim:** “First concrete engineering blueprint” is a historical priority claim that should be compared
  with earlier ion/atom/cavity proposals and sourced.
- **Current factual overclaim:** Trapped ions do not unconditionally hold both highest-fidelity gates and longest
  coherence across every metric/platform; nuclear spins and specialized memories complicate the claim.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.14 NMR demonstrations

- **Important conceptual omission:** Liquid-state NMR “qubits” use highly mixed ensemble states and did not
  generally demonstrate scalable entanglement; the legitimacy of calling some experiments quantum computation was
  debated. This belongs in any precise historical assessment.
- **Causal overclaim:** Control techniques did influence later work, but “transferred to later platforms” should
  name pulse/NMR concepts rather than imply direct hardware inheritance.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §0.14 DiVincenzo criteria

- **Positive:** The deployment-checklist analogy is excellent.
- **Overstatement:** A vendor may credibly pursue analog simulation, annealing, sensing, or a modular architecture
  without satisfying all five locally; “selling something other than a universal quantum computer” is fair only
  after specifying that target.
- **Criterion 1:** Useful computational subsystems need not be literal two-level physical systems; leakage-managed
  multilevel encodings are common.
- **Criterion 3:** $T_\text{coh}/T_\text{gate}$ alone is not a reliable gate budget under stochastic gate error,
  crosstalk, leakage, idling, and QEC cycles.
