  exact molecule/visibility/source; atom count and mass are different measures.
- **Overgeneralization:** “Every system tested” is unbounded; say increasingly massive systems under coherence-
  preserving conditions.
- **Tonomura categorization:** Electron biprism work is more directly discussed under double-slit/interference;
  calling it Mach–Zehnder reproduction may be inaccurate.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.3 what superposition does not mean

- **False dichotomy:** Rejecting “a wave that spreads through both paths” is too categorical; the wavefunction/
  quantum field amplitude does propagate and wave descriptions are operationally valid, though not a classical
  material wave.
- **Detector clicks:** Discrete photodetection does not by itself rule out classical wave fields; semiclassical
  photodetection and nonclassical correlations must be distinguished.
- **“Third kind”:** Engaging but interpretation-laden; say neither classical ignorance mixture nor classical
  divisible wave is adequate.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.4 opening

- **Model scope:** Amplitudes add for coherent alternatives corresponding to the same final event; they do not
  simply “meet at a detector” in all formulations.
- **Overclaim:** “Source of every ... speedup” is a recurring unsupported universal. Complexity advantage depends
  on structure, access, entanglement/contextuality in some models, and resource accounting.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.4 Tonomura experiment

- **Historical naming issue:** Tonomura's electron biprism demonstration is often called a double-slit-like
  experiment, not literal narrow slits. The text says “equivalent,” which is responsible.
- **“Independently emitted”:** Ensure source intensity/coherence conditions support negligible overlap; electrons
  are indistinguishable fermions but events can be temporally separated.
- **Positive:** Single-event accumulation is vividly described.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.4 wave-particle account

- **Interpretive caution:** “Amplitude propagates, detection is discrete” is an operational summary, not a
  resolution of every wave-particle/ontology question.
- **QFT nuance:** Photodetection discreteness is naturally explained by quantized fields; single-particle
  wavefunction language has limits.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.4 algorithm analogy

- **Overgeneralization:** Many algorithms estimate phases, sample distributions, simulate dynamics, or transform
  observables without a clean wanted/wrong-answer amplification narrative.
- **Cross-reference defect:** QFT is mathematical background in §4.13 and algorithmic use in Chapters 14/15; the
  phrase “QFT (Chapter 14)” is plausible but verify TOC section.
- **Causal overstatement:** Algorithms do not exist *because* Mach–Zehnder clicks; both instantiate linear
  amplitude evolution.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.4 indistinguishability/decoherence

- **Technical defect:** “Even in principle” is too absolute. Interference visibility depends quantitatively on
  overlap of environment/path-marker states; partial which-path distinguishability yields partial visibility, and
  quantum erasure can restore conditional fringes.
- **Human observation:** Correctly notes no conscious observer is needed.
- **Rule precision:** Add amplitudes for indistinguishable alternatives and probabilities for orthogonally recorded
  alternatives; intermediate overlap produces partial coherence.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.5 measurement definition

- **Definition too broad:** Not every system–apparatus interaction producing a record is a measurement *of the
  intended system observable*; a quantum instrument specifies outcome probabilities and conditional channels.
- **Basis defect:** General measurements are not described merely by a basis or vague “collection of outcomes.”
  POVM effects need not be orthogonal/rank-one, and the apparatus need not report a basis element the system
  “ended up in.”
- **State-update conflation:** Outcome probabilities and post-measurement state are separate parts of an instrument;
  a POVM alone does not determine the latter.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §3.5 Stern–Gerlach measurement example

- **Physical idealization:** A spatially separating Stern–Gerlach stage plus filtering/detection is not necessarily
  a nondestructive apparatus from which the same atom “emerges” in a usable eigenstate.
- **Positive:** Same space/different rotated basis is a clear qubit connection.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.5 Born frequencies and measurement problem

- **Statistical precision:** Relative-frequency convergence is probabilistic (law-of-large-numbers behavior), not
  an exact finite-ensemble equality.
- **Pure-state limitation:** General Born rule uses $\mathrm{tr}(\rho E_m)`.
- **Interpretation:** The measurement problem is broader than “why one outcome”; it includes definite outcomes,
  preferred basis, and unitary measurement dynamics.
- **Positive:** Correctly brackets metaphysics from computational prediction.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.5 “no fixed value” implication

- **Interpretational overclaim:** Operational QM does not assign a premeasurement $S_z$ eigenvalue, but “there is
  no preexisting answer” is an ontological claim. Contextual/nonlocal hidden-variable theories complicate it.
- **Analogy defect:** A classical 45° vector has well-defined projections onto horizontal/vertical axes; asking
  whether it *is* horizontal is not analogous to stochastic spin outcomes and may misteach projection.
- **Qualifier:** “Most observables” lacks a measure/meaning; say a state is generally not an eigenstate of a chosen
  observable.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.5 basis-dependent statistics

- **Positive:** Excellent $|+\rangle` example and clear statement that measurement choice is experimental context.
- **Terminology:** An individual outcome is random; “state is not random” conflates pure-state preparation with
  deterministic outcome in one basis. Say measurement is deterministic in the X basis.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.6 opening collapse account

- **False universal:** Quantum measurement does have classical analogues with back-action (probing a small system,
  Bayesian conditioning plus physical disturbance); what lacks a classical analogue is the specific irreducible
  state-update/incompatibility structure.
- **Degeneracy defect:** After a projective outcome the state lies in the outcome eigenspace, not necessarily “the
  basis state”; Lüders update handles degeneracy.
- **Instrument dependence:** General measurements need not prepare the eigenstate or be repeatable.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.6 sequential same-axis Stern–Gerlach

- **Idealization:** Probability-one repeatability assumes ideal projective filtering, no precession/decoherence,
  identical alignment, and surviving particles.
- **Positive:** The preparation-by-measurement idea is concrete.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.6 inserted x measurement

- **Postselection:** “Select $|\uparrow_x\rangle$ output” discards half the particles; clarify conditioning so
  readers do not confuse measurement with deterministic re-preparation.
- **Anthropomorphism:** The spin has not “forgotten” a possessed value; the intermediate operation changes the
  state so the later distribution is uniform.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.6 incompatible observables

- **Causal defect:** Incompatibility is not defined because “measurements physically disturb each other”; it is
  noncommutativity/no joint sharp measurement (with nuances). Disturbance is one operational consequence.
- **Common-eigenstate nuance:** Noncommuting observables can share some eigenvectors in dimensions above two, so
  incompatibility statements require care.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.6 unknowable disturbance bullet

- **Interpretational overclaim:** “Not by anything ... known in advance” rules out hidden variables more broadly
  than standard operational QM and Bell results warrant. Say the quantum state provides no deterministic
  prediction of the outcome.
- **Outcome-conditioned state:** Correct under ideal projective instrument.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §3.6 non-clumsy apparatus bullet

- **Precision:** There are weak/unsharp/joint approximate measurements trading information and disturbance; no
  perfect sharp $S_x$ measurement preserves every $S_z$ state, but “no refined apparatus” is too broad.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.6 mathematical compression

- **Major mathematical defect:** Operators that fail to commute do **not** generally have mutually unbiased
  eigenbases. Pauli X/Z do, but generic noncommuting observables need not.
- **Major overgeneralization:** Measuring in one basis does not generally randomize a state prepared in another;
  uniform outcomes occur only for mutually unbiased bases.
- **Cross-reference suspicion:** Commutators are defined in §4.4/§4.5 depending structure; verify exact location.
- **Positive:** Final interaction-not-passive-reading takeaway survives after qualification.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.7 Robertson relation

- **Mathematical defect:** A nonzero operator commutator does not guarantee a nonzero lower bound in every state;
  $\langle[A,B]\rangle` can vanish. The premise should not imply all such pairs always have positive uncertainty
  product bounds.
- **Spin defect:** For spin components the lower bound is state-dependent, e.g.
  $\Delta S_x\Delta S_z\ge(\hbar/2)|\langle S_y\rangle|`, not simply “bounded below by a constant.”
- **Domain:** Unbounded position/momentum operators require domain qualifications.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.7 apparatus versus state

- **Overstatement:** “You can measure either to arbitrary precision” ignores finite resources and measurement-
  disturbance/energy constraints; the principle itself does not impose instrumental resolution, but physics may.
- **Positive:** Preparation uncertainty versus colloquial instrument error is clearly distinguished.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.7 spin intuition

- **Positive:** Exact 50/50 X statistics for a Z eigenstate is correct.
- **Interpretational wording:** “No classical answer” should be “no quantum-state assignment of a pre-existing
  sharp value”; hidden-variable interpretations require nuance.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.7 simultaneous measurement and VQE

- **Technical defect:** A qubit's X and Z cannot be jointly **sharply** measured, but approximate joint/unsharp
  measurements exist.
- **VQE defect:** Hamiltonian terms need not be grouped only by operator commutation; qubit-wise commutation,
  general commuting measurements, basis-rotation circuits, classical shadows, and joint-measurement schemes offer
  alternatives.
- **Cross-reference:** VQE placement in Chapter 15 should be verified against TOC.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.8 definition

- **Pure-state limitation:** “Cannot be written as a product” correctly defines pure-state entanglement, but mixed
  states are entangled when not expressible as a convex mixture of product states.
- **Overclaim:** Entanglement is not the source of **every** beyond-classical correlation protocol; prepare-and-
  measure contextuality/nonorthogonality can matter without shared entanglement.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.8 Bell state local and same-basis correlations

- **Major conceptual defect:** Perfect same-basis correlations *are* reducible to a shared classical random bit.
  Nonclassicality appears only in the joint family of correlations across suitably chosen settings violating Bell
  inequalities. A later sentence partly says this but the earlier absolute is false.
- **Basis precision:** For $|\Phi^+\rangle`, same outcomes occur for bases related by real rotations; arbitrary
  common complex basis gives conjugate-basis structure, and Y anticorrelates as correctly noted.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.8 SPDC mechanism

- **Physical defect:** It is misleading to narrate SPDC as a single pump photon literally “splits” with definite
  event history; the nonlinear interaction converts pump-field excitations into correlated modes under energy/
  momentum phase matching.
- **Material superlative:** BBO is common, not universally “the standard”; PPKTP and integrated nonlinear
  platforms are also standard.
- **Rate defect:** Pairs/s/mW is meaningless without bandwidth, collection efficiency, detector efficiency, pump
  regime, and brightness definition. The stale-anchor audit already flags this claim.
- **False universal:** “Essentially every optical Bell test” excludes atomic cascade, quantum dots, four-wave
  mixing, entangled atoms, and other sources.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.8 Bell theorem summary

- **Assumption precision:** Bell locality is not merely particles carrying fixed variables unable to communicate;
  factorization/local causality and measurement independence matter. Stochastic models are included.
- **Entangled-state qualifier:** Not every entangled state/settings violate a Bell inequality; some mixed entangled
  states admit local models for certain measurements.
- **Tsirelson:** $2\sqrt2` is the CHSH maximum, not “that inequality” for every Bell inequality.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.8 Aspect and loophole history

- **Historical defect risk:** Aspect's switching was periodic/deterministic and did not fully close locality under
  modern criteria; “faster than light could travel” is oversimplified.
- **Freedom-of-choice defect:** It is not closed in the same sense as detection/locality; cosmic and human-choice
  experiments push common-cause assumptions back rather than eliminate superdeterminism.
- **2015 wording:** “Closed ... freedom-of-choice” overstates assumptions; the Prelude itself gives a more careful
  account and should be canonicalized.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.8 conclusions

- **“Empirically real”:** Bell violation empirically establishes nonclassical correlations under experimental
  assumptions, while entanglement identification may depend on device/model trust. The broad point is sound.
- **No-signaling precision:** Local reduced-state statistics are unchanged by remote **trace-preserving** choices
  when outcomes are not conditioned; postselection/classical communication changes conditional ensembles.
- **Resource overclaim:** Teleportation/dense coding require entanglement plus quantum/classical communication and
  task accounting; device-independent QKD additionally requires loophole-free behavior/security assumptions.
- **Cross-reference defect:** Teleportation is likely not developed simply in Chapter 7's resource section; verify
  exact chapter/figure link.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.9 moving-target warning and opening

- **Status:** May 2026 figures require re-verification as of July 19, 2026; “best sources” are not locally cited.
- **Mechanism precision:** Environment entanglement reduces local coherence when conditional environment states
  become distinguishable; it does not invariably destroy *all* interference.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.9 operational definition

- **Major defect:** Decoherence need not make a state indistinguishable from the exact mixture with weights
  $|\alpha|^2,|\beta|^2`; partial decoherence leaves off-diagonal terms, and nonorthogonal pointer states/basis
  selection matter.
- **“Any measurement”:** Exact indistinguishability for every system-only measurement occurs only after complete
  dephasing to the same reduced density operator.
- **Recoverability:** Information recovery may be impossible in practice and not always meaningful under Markovian
  approximations; “in principle” assumes access/control of purification.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.9 T1/T2 definitions

- **Technical nuance:** $T_2$ contains relaxation and pure dephasing:
  $1/T_2=1/(2T_1)+1/T_\phi` under common Markovian models; echo/Ramsey $T_2$ differ.
- **False universal:** $T_2\le2T_1` assumes standard two-level Markovian relaxation/dephasing models.
- **Priority:** Which time is practically relevant depends on gates, dynamical decoupling, leakage, and error
  correction; $T_1$ can dominate too.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §3.9 platform times

- **Overbroad/false claims:** Neutral-atom coherence is not generically comparable to ions; photonic states do
  suffer dephasing/mode decoherence as well as loss; NV numbers conflate electron/nuclear spins and control
  protocols.
- **Comparability:** Ramsey, echo, memory, idle, transport, and logical coherence times are not interchangeable.
- **Stale evidence:** This section has moving-target claims but no nearby citations.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §3.9 coherence/gate ratio and QEC

- **Major metric defect:** Coherence-time/gate-time is not “how many operations” can reliably be applied; gate
  infidelity, correlated noise, leakage, crosstalk, parallelism, and control errors matter.
- **QEC description:** QEC suppresses logical error; saying it “stretches coherence time” is an intuitive outcome,
  not its complete function.
- **Cross-reference defect:** “Chapter 19 and Part IX” mixes QEC in Part VIII with hardware/control Part IX; okay as
  two destinations but ambiguous.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.9 unitary dilation conclusion

- **Overstatement:** Not every open-system channel at every time admits one fixed environment/unitary with the
  simple physical interpretation given, though Stinespring dilation exists per CPTP map.
- **Decoherence versus dissipation:** Open dynamics includes relaxation and noise beyond loss of interference;
  entanglement is not the only narrative in non-Markovian/classical-noise models.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.10 closed/open setup

- **Postulate mismatch:** Chapter 5 includes measurement, density matrices, reduced states, and partial trace—not
  only closed systems.
- **Absolute isolation:** Truly closed systems are idealizations; useful.
- **Engineering priority:** Control/open-system gap is central but not singular; scalable fabrication/readout/
  decoding also matter.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.10 larger-system picture

- **Conceptual overreach:** Declaring the total system+environment closed and unitary can recurse indefinitely and
  is an ideal modeling assumption, not directly observed fact.
- **Reference defect:** Quantum channels are developed in Chapter 10 according to the project reconciliation note,
  not only open systems in Chapter 18; link the actual introduction.
- **Positive:** Density matrix/partial trace signpost is appropriate.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.10 classical limit/decoherence

- **Major conceptual defect:** Fast decoherence alone does not derive classical mechanics, definite outcomes, or
  classical probabilities; pointer states, dynamics, coarse graining, initial conditions, and interpretation
  remain.
- **Ontological overclaim:** “Superpositions exist in principle” is interpretation-dependent.
- **Macroscopic contact:** Not all macroscopic systems are in “thermal contact” in the same sense; scattering and
  internal degrees cause decoherence.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.10 action-scale heuristic

- **Numeric precision:** $\hbar=1.054...\times10^{-34}$ J·s, so one-significant-figure approximation is acceptable.
- **Overgeneralization:** Action $\gg\hbar` is not sufficient for classicality; chaos, interference, coherent
  collective states, low temperature, and decoherence conditions matter.
- **Quantum-computing defect:** Qubits are not kept quantum because “action scale ... comparable to $\hbar$” alone;
  level isolation, low entropy, control, and coherence are decisive.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.10 wave-particle closing

- **Pure-state overreach:** A quantum system is not always described by a state vector; mixed states/fields.
- **Interpretive claim:** “State vector is the underlying object” is an ontological commitment inconsistent with
  the book's interpretation-agnostic promise.
- **Particle detection:** Discrete detector records arise from system+detector interaction/QFT, not simply an
  intrinsic particle aspect.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.11 summary

- **Measurement defect:** Again limits measurement to basis projection.
- **Speedup overclaim:** Interference is not a complete causal explanation of all advantage.
- **Bell wording:** Bell tests rule out local hidden-variable models under assumptions; classical joint
  distributions can reproduce any single fixed-setting correlation.
- **Decoherence:** Not the dominant constraint on *every* hardware item; loss, control, leakage, fabrication, and
  source/detector efficiency may dominate.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §3.11 chapter map

- **Content defect:** Chapter 4 may introduce partial trace only lightly while Chapter 5 develops it; verify.
- **Transmon:** A transmon is multilevel and used as an approximate/encoded two-level subsystem, not “any other
  two-level system.”
- **Bloch sphere:** It will not be relied on “for the rest of book” for multiqubit/mixed-state generality.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.11 sanity check 1

- **Positive:** Correct conditional probability under ideal projective/postselected sequence.
- **Wording:** “Destroys information” should be “changes state so later Z outcome is uniform.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.11 sanity check 2

- **Defect:** A generic nondestructive path detector may yield partial distinguishability; equal 1/2 outputs assume
  perfect which-path marking, balanced beam splitters, ignored marker outcomes, and ideal alignment.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.11 sanity check 3

- **Defect:** The Bell state/polarization correlation depends on which Bell state and basis convention. It says “a
  Bell pair” without specifying correlated versus anticorrelated state.
- **Positive:** No-signaling local marginal takeaway is correct.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.11 sanity check 4

- **Interpretation defect:** “Superpositions exist ... unobservable” is not an operationally settled statement;
  decoherence suppresses local interference but does not solve outcome ontology.
- **Atom count:** Coffee-cup $10^{25}$ is an uncited rough value and unnecessary.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §3.11 sanity check 5

- **Correctness:** The stated state is +45° linear polarization under standard H/V phase convention; ideal +45°
  polarizer transmits with probability one.
- **Device qualification:** Real polarizers are lossy/imperfect, but ideal exercise is clear.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### Sanity-check format, navigation, and Chapter 3 verdict

- **Sanctioned exception:** Answers inline are explicitly allowed for Chapter 3 by STYLE; formatting is a numbered
  list in the bridge as required, though the lead says “sanity-checks” rather than exact bold run-in convention.
- **Navigation:** Correct.
- **Overall:** Exceptionally vivid physical intuition, but it repeatedly crosses from operational statements into
  ontology, idealizes measurements/platforms as perfect, and universalizes special Pauli/projective examples.
- **Strong-defect shortlist:** noncommuting⇒mutually-unbiased error; arbitrary incompatible measurement⇒random
  error; spin Stern–Gerlach atom/electron conflation; complex amplitudes uniquely forced claim; perfect PBS claim;
  Bell same-basis correlations claimed nonclassical; freedom-of-choice closure; complete-decoherence mixture
  universalization; classical limit reduced to decoherence; and state vector called underlying ontology.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**
- **Second-pass status:** Complete for this file; all sections/footer read separately.

### `book/part-02-formalism/04-mathematical-background.md` — second pass

#### Header and refresher contract

- **Positive:** “Make every formula type-check in your head” is excellent audience-specific framing.
- **Reference usability:** Strang/Axler/Nielsen–Chuang serve different levels/conventions; attach exact chapters or
  map each to the gap it repairs.
- **Scope mismatch:** The chapter later derives/introduces substantial SVD, partial trace, QFT, Holevo, and
  amplitude-estimation material; “only refresher” may understate cognitive load.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### Finite-dimensional and convention assumptions

- **Internal contradiction:** The chapter says everything is finite-dimensional, then uses position/momentum and
  uncertainty elsewhere/adjacent chapters; this chapter should say its core development is finite-dimensional and
  explicitly flag formal excursions.
- **Continuous-variable destination:** Chapter 32 is not the only place infinite-dimensional/bosonic systems
  appear; hardware, photonics, Hamiltonians, and Chapter 5 Schrödinger dynamics invoke them.
- **Inner-product warning:** Clear and essential.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### Reading guide

- **Dependency suspicion:** SVD is foundational for QSVT/block encodings but skim advice is fine; QFT basics may be
  needed before Chapter 14/15 rather than immediately.
- **Terminology:** “Holevo/amplitude-estimation preview in §4.14” risks overcrowding a probability refresher and
  may duplicate Chapters 12/15.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.1 complex-number definitions

- **Precision:** Polar form at $z=0$ has undefined/nonunique argument; specify $z\ne0` or arbitrary angle at zero.
- **Branch:** $\arg z` is multivalued or requires a principal branch; beginner refresher can note modulo $2\pi`.
- **Positive:** Rotation interpretation is concise.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.1 amplitudes and Dirac notation

- **Pure-state scope:** A qubit can be mixed; this defines a pure qubit state and should say so.
- **Global phase geometry:** “Unit-length vector” includes physically equivalent vectors; projective-ray caveat
  arrives later but belongs near the definition.
- **Label defect:** “Symbol inside $|\;\rangle$ is a label, nothing more” is too categorical. Kets may contain
  expressions/operators/continuous indices and the notation denotes a vector; basis labels are labels in these
  examples.
- **Born-rule scope:** Explicitly says computational measurement, good.
- **Overclaim:** Interference is important but “the engine of speedups” remains a slogan rather than theorem.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.1 amplitudes versus probabilities and general measurements

- **Positive:** This is substantially more precise than Chapters 1–3: measurement is specified and POVMs are
  foreshadowed.
- **Wording:** Squared moduli do not “become” probabilities generically; a measurement's inner products/effects
  produce probabilities.
- **Classical nuance:** Quasiprobabilities and signed measures exist classically as representations, though literal
  event probabilities remain nonnegative.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.1 global phase

- **Proof incompleteness:** Showing computational-basis component magnitudes are unchanged does not alone prove
  global phase cancels from **every** measurement. Show
  $\rho=|\psi\rangle\langle\psi|` is unchanged or
  $|\langle m|e^{i\theta}\psi\rangle|^2` for arbitrary $m`.
- **Qualification:** Global phase can become a relative phase when comparing controlled branches/phase references;
  the global phase of a complete isolated state remains unobservable.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.1 relative phase and Hadamard example

- **Correctness:** The $|+\rangle/|-\rangle` transformation is correct and pedagogically ideal.
- **Terminology:** The states are distinguishable in other bases before applying a physical H; H followed by Z
  measurement implements X-basis measurement.
- **Cross-reference suspicion:** §4.5 may discuss operator classes rather than introduce H matrix; verify exact
  first definition.
- **Style:** “Large fraction” is unverifiable but harmless voice.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §4.2 vector-space definitions

- **Completeness:** Referring to “standard eight axioms” without listing/linking them is fine for a refresher but
  makes the section non-self-contained for selective readers.
- **Subspace wording:** The span is a subspace of $V`, not necessarily “the subspace” without ambient declaration.
- **Basis size:** Dimension is cardinality for finite bases; scope already finite.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.2 qubit space and rays

- **Positive:** This repairs earlier chapters by correctly distinguishing ambient vectors, normalized
  representatives, rays, and global phase.
- **Mathematical nuance:** A ray is usually a one-dimensional subspace/equivalence under nonzero scalar; physical
  pure states can equivalently be rank-one projectors.
- **System assumption:** $n$ qubits have tensor-product Hilbert space only once distinguishable subsystem structure
  is specified; identical-particle encodings can require symmetric/antisymmetric subspaces.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.2 normalization convention

- **Notation defect:** Writing `$|v\rangle/\|v\|$` after defining $v` as a mathematical vector overloads ket
  notation slightly; acceptable but `$v/\|v\|$` is cleaner until the basis map is chosen.
- **Operational caveat:** Unnormalized vectors are useful for postselection/conditional states; “ket as state
  assumed normalized” can hide this later.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.2 exponential growth

- **Positive:** Explicitly says dimension alone does not yield speedup.
- **Precision:** “Explains why generic simulation is hard” still promotes representation size into causal proof.
  Say it explains dense-statevector cost and motivates complexity questions.
- **Restricted measurements:** This is an important but unexplained ingredient; a sentence on limited readout would
  help.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.2 computational basis and ordering

- **Positive:** Mapping, index formula, and tensor order are explicit.
- **Notation:** Omitting `\otimes` in `$|x_1\rangle|x_2\rangle$` is conventional only after stated; §4.8 has not yet
  introduced abbreviation. Include it here once.
- **Framework warning:** “Opposite convention” oversimplifies frameworks that distinguish qubit labels,
  tensor-factor order, classical-bit display, diagram order, and integer endianness.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.2 sanity check

- **Defect:** “Mismatch ... is framework convention, not a bug” could also be a user's mapping/serialization bug.
  Encourage explicit basis-action verification rather than pre-judging.
- **Positive:** Concrete index 2 check is valuable.
- **Format:** Inline blockquoted sanity checks are not chapter-closing checks; STYLE permits definitions/asides but
  may confuse the sanctioned closing convention.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.3 inner product and norm

- **Correctness:** Formula and conjugate-linearity convention are correct.
- **Style overclaim:** Mathematics literature is not uniform and quantum literature also sometimes varies in
  mathematical subfields/software; “is consistent” is too absolute.
- **Norm:** State explicitly positive definiteness makes $\|v\|=0` iff $v=0`.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.3 notation inventory

- **Positive:** The modulus/norm/operator-absolute-value distinction prevents real errors.
- **Trace-norm definition:** Matrix square root is not introduced yet; either forward-link positive square roots
  (§4.5/4.7) or mark lookup-only.
- **Scope:** Operator norm needs domain/codomain inner products; finite square operators are assumed.
- **Potential missing norm:** Hilbert–Schmidt/Frobenius norm is in notation front matter but absent here despite
  likely later use; consistency check.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.3 syntax explanation

- **Wording:** “Syntactically distinct” is reader notation, not guaranteed parser syntax; Markdown parsers may be
  exactly where bars conflict.
- **Positive:** `$ \|A\|_{\mathrm{op}}$` disambiguation is good.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.3 orthonormal decomposition

- **Correctness:** Coefficient order matches physics convention.
- **Gram–Schmidt defect:** It converts any **ordered linearly independent finite list/basis** into an orthonormal
  basis; “any basis” is fine in finite dimension but fails numerically for nearly dependent vectors and requires
  order.
- **Terminology:** The expansion is not “the projection ... onto each basis ket” as a singular object; each term is
  a component/projection and their sum resolves identity.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.3 takeaway and sanity check

- **Conceptual defect:** Conjugation in one slot plus positive definiteness makes self-inner-products nonnegative;
  conjugation alone does not.
- **Example:** Correct and useful.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.3 Cauchy–Schwarz

- **Edge case:** Equality iff linearly dependent includes either zero vector; correct under standard definition.
- **Forward value:** Mentioning fidelity/measurement bounds motivates the refresher, but a tiny example would make
  the payoff more tangible.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.4 operator and matrix representation

- **Scope:** A linear map can be $V\to W`; “operator” commonly means endomorphism, so the chosen scope is fine but
  should distinguish rectangular maps used in channels/isometries.
- **Basis formula:** Correct for orthonormal basis; in a nonorthonormal basis dual-basis/Gram factors enter.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.4 adjoint

- **Correctness:** Matrix and basis-independent definitions agree under the stated physics convention.
- **Scope:** Existence/uniqueness is automatic finite-dimensionally; useful to remind readers because unbounded
  operators later are subtler.
- **Terminology:** “Hermitian transpose” can be confused with a Hermitian operator; “conjugate transpose” is safest.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.4 identity list

- **Correctness:** Listed adjoint/determinant/trace identities are correct for compatible finite square matrices.
- **Cyclic trace caveat:** “Any cyclic permutation whose dimensions match” is good; explicitly warn trace is not
  invariant under arbitrary reordering.
- **Basis independence:** Trace/determinant are similarity invariants for endomorphisms, not entries independent of
  choosing arbitrary unrelated domain/codomain bases.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.4 identity and projectors

- **Cross-reference defect:** Orthogonal projectors “reappear as measurement operators in §4.6,” but spectral
  decomposition is §4.7 and measurement postulates Chapter 5; §4.6 is eigenvalues/eigenvectors.
- **Terminology:** In mathematical usage “projection” usually means idempotent and “orthogonal projection” adds
  self-adjointness; the paragraph handles this well.
- **Physical nuance:** Projective-measurement operations include outcome probabilities/state-update/Kraus maps;
  a projector alone represents an effect/subspace, not the whole measurement instrument.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.4 projector sanity check

- **Correctness:** $P` and $Q` examples work; $Q` maps $(x,y)` to $(x+y,0)` along kernel span $(-1,1)`, clarifying
  direction could enhance intuition.
- **Formatting:** Display math nested in a blockquote should be visually checked under GitHub/mdBook/Pandoc.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.4 Hilbert–Schmidt inner product

- **Scope:** For rectangular operators of the same shape this also works; current endomorphism scope is enough.
- **Positive:** Operator-as-vector framing prepares Liouville/superoperator ideas.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.4 pure expectation

- **Terminology:** Expectation requires normalized $|\psi\rangle` and Hermitian observable; both are stated across
  adjacent sentences.
- **Selective-reader issue:** Bra-ket sandwich precedes full Dirac grammar by eight sections; the right-to-left
  explanation helps.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.4 density expectation and HS framing

- **Technical precision:** $\mathrm{tr}(\rho O)=\langle\rho,O\rangle_{\rm HS}`
  because $\rho^\dagger=\rho`; correct.
- **Potential conceptual trap:** Calling a non-Hermitian expectation an “operator overlap” is okay, but non-
  Hermitian operators can represent measurable combinations through Hermitian/anti-Hermitian parts.
- **Fidelity note:** Root fidelity conventions differ; warning that it is not HS inner product is useful.
- **Consistency:** HS norm omitted in §4.3 but introduced here, explaining the earlier inventory gap.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.5 Hermitian operators

- **Correctness:** Finite-dimensional spectral properties are stated correctly.
- **Observable nuance:** Physical observables correspond to self-adjoint operators, but not every Hermitian matrix
  is tied to a directly implemented laboratory measurement without a measurement procedure.
- **Infinite-dimensional destination defect:** Position/momentum machinery is not simply postponed to Chapter 32;
  Chapter 5 uses Schrödinger/Hamiltonian language. Flag formal use versus rigorous treatment.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.5 unitary operators

- **Correctness:** Equivalences and eigenvalue modulus are correct in finite dimensions.
- **Positive:** Explicitly repairs earlier chapters by naming reset/noise/channels as nonunitary.
- **Nuance:** Time evolution “between gates” may include control Hamiltonians and noise; only ideal isolated
  evolution is unitary, already qualified.
- **Cross-reference:** Channel placement in Chapter 10 matches reconciliation note.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.5 normal operators

- **Correctness:** Finite-dimensional spectral theorem statement is correct.
- **Polish:** Give one normal-but-neither-Hermitian-nor-unitary example (e.g. diagonal `diag(1,2i)`) to prove
  converse failure concretely.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.**

#### §4.5 PSD definition and equivalence

- **Proof circularity:** The equivalence proof expands in an eigenbasis before spectral theorem is established in
  §4.7. This is acceptable preview but logically depends on the very theorem introduced later; mark it.
- **Strict positivity:** Distinguish positive definite `$A\succ0$` from semidefinite for later inverses/logs.
- **Bra-ket spacing:** `\langle v | A | v\rangle` is consistent enough but differs from compact style elsewhere.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.5 PSD applications

- **POVM effect bound:** $0\preceq E_i\preceq I` follows from positivity plus completeness; correct for a complete
  POVM.
- **Terminology:** “Canonical square roots live in this cone” is evocative but define uniqueness of the PSD square
  root after spectral theorem.
- **Fidelity:** Fidelity conventions (squared/unsquared) will matter; not yet an error.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.5 gate matrices and property list

- **Correctness:** Matrices/properties are correct.
- **Z wording:** It does not “flip the relative phase of any superposition” if $\beta=0` or if “flip” implies a
  unique phase sign; it adds $\pi` to the relative phase of the $|1\rangle` component.
- **H basis:** Maps Z basis to X basis; inverse also maps X to Z because $H^2=I`.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.5 sanity checks

- **Format inconsistency:** “Sanity checks.” appears as plain run-in text, followed later by a singular blockquote.
  These are local exercises, but recurring use of the sanctioned closing label risks hierarchy confusion.
- **Exercises:** Correct and appropriately small.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.5 commutators and simultaneous diagonalization

- **Correctness:** Pairwise commuting normal finite-dimensional family is simultaneously unitarily diagonalizable.
- **Compatibility nuance:** Joint measurability extends beyond commuting PVMs for POVMs; “compatible observables”
  here must mean sharp/projective observables.
- **Noncommuting nuance:** “Generally do not” is careful; noncommuting matrices can share some eigenvectors without
  a full common basis.
- **Cross-reference:** Trotterization §16.2 should be checked against current Chapter 16 headings.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.5 Pauli commutators/products

- **Correctness:** Signs and products are correct under standard Pauli convention.
- **Pedagogy:** This directly corrects Chapter 3's vague “order matters” with a concrete algebraic amount.
- **Polish:** Add anticommutators `$\\{X,Y\\}=0$` because measurement grouping/QEC later use both relations.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.6 eigenvalue definition and characteristic polynomial

- **Correctness:** Definitions and algebraic-multiplicity count over $\mathbb C` are correct.
- **Scope:** “Eigenvalues are roots” applies square finite matrices; already scoped but worth keeping explicit.
- **Numerical caution:** Characteristic-polynomial root finding is rarely a stable computational eigenvalue method;
  an experienced-developer sidebar could prevent implementation misuse.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.6 operator-class spectral properties

- **Logical ordering:** This invokes the finite-dimensional spectral theorem before §4.7 formally develops it; the
  chapter is pedagogically recursive. Say “as proved next.”
- **Unitary notation:** Writing the eigenvalue set as `$\\{e^{i\theta_k}\\}$` may imply distinct values; multiplicity
  remains possible.
- **Correctness:** Claims are otherwise correct.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.6 eigenspaces and multiplicities

- **Set-brace source:** Renderer-safe doubled braces are used.
- **Zero vector:** Eigenspace correctly includes zero even though eigenvectors exclude it.
- **Normal claim:** Algebraic/geometric equality and spanning hold; degeneracy is handled.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.**

#### §4.6 projective measurement with degeneracy

- **Correctness:** Born probability and Lüders conditional state are correct for ideal Lüders instruments.
- **Instrument nuance:** A projective measurement of a degenerate observable need not implement the Lüders update;
  it can refine/disturb within the eigenspace while having the same outcome PVM. Label the update as the standard
  Lüders rule assumed by the book.
- **Terminology:** A projector is an outcome **effect**, while the conditional map `$P_\lambda\rho P_\lambda$`
  specifies the operation; “measurement-outcome operator” is ambiguous.
- **Zero outcome:** Saying it has no post-measurement state is sensible; regular conditional conventions can assign
  arbitrary states on null events, but irrelevant here.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.6 nondegenerate rank-one case

- **Correctness:** Probability formula and post-state up to phase are correct under ideal rank-one projection.
- **Phase:** With the explicit Lüders update the normalized state is
  `$e^{i\arg\langle b_i|\psi\rangle}|b_i\rangle$`; “up to global phase” is apt.
- **Positive:** This is a very effective bridge from abstract PVMs to circuit recipes.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.6 sanity check

- **Edge case:** If $\alpha=0`, the requested post-measurement state conditioned on outcome 0 is undefined because
  the outcome has zero probability. The exercise should explicitly assume $\alpha\ne0` or ask the reader to
  identify the zero-probability case.
- **Expected result:** Probability $|\alpha|^2`; conditioned state $|0\rangle` up to phase.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.7 spectral-decomposition statement

- **Correctness:** The projector-valued decomposition and orthogonality/completeness identities are correct for
  finite-dimensional normal operators.
- **Terminology:** “Distinct spectral values” is redundant but helpfully disambiguated from multiplicity.
- **Index collision:** Later matrix-exponential power-series index `$k$` reuses the number of spectral values $k`;
  harmless but locally avoidable.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.7 projector ranks and multiplicities

- **Correctness:** Normality guarantees algebraic=geometric multiplicity; rank-one formula needs normalized
  eigenvector and is correctly qualified.
- **Pedagogy:** Explicitly stating degeneracy prevents the common false assumption that every spectral projector is
  an outer product.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.7 functional calculus

- **Nuance:** For a finite spectrum, arbitrary assigned values define $f(A)`; no continuity/analyticity is needed.
  The statement is correct.
- **Extension:** If two functions agree on the spectrum they define the same operator; worth saying because branch
  choices later are spectral choices.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.7 time evolution

- **Correctness:** Time-independent formula and $\hbar=1` convention are correct.
- **Overstatement:** “Simulating dynamics reduces to diagonalizing H when feasible” is one route, not an algorithmic
  reduction in general; direct exponentiation/product formulas/Krylov methods avoid full diagonalization.
- **Chapter reference:** Chapter 16 is correct for Hamiltonian simulation; time-dependent treatment should be
  verified rather than promised vaguely.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.7 square roots and inverses

- **Terminology:** “Positive semidefinite Hermitian” is redundant under the book's definition but reinforces scope.
- **Square-root nuance:** Every PSD matrix has a unique PSD square root, though it can have other non-PSD square
  roots; call it the unique PSD square root.
- **HHL reference defect risk:** §15.5 should be checked against current headings.
- **HHL precision:** HHL does not literally implement a deterministic `$A^{-1}|\!b\rangle$`; it prepares a
  normalized state proportional to the inverse action probabilistically/with amplitude amplification and
  condition-number dependence. “Quantum implementation” overstates.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.7 Hermitian functions and generators

- **Technical defect:** If `$f$ has unit-modulus values on the spectrum, $f(A)$ is unitary` holds for normal $A`
  with a complete spectral decomposition; the paragraph begins “when A is Hermitian,” so fine.
- **One-parameter group nuance:** $e^{-itA}` is a strongly continuous unitary group in finite dimensions and every
  Hermitian generator works; physical Hamiltonian dimensions require $\hbar`.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.7 matrix exponential

- **Correctness:** Power series and normal spectral evaluation are correct.
- **False practice claim:** Hamiltonian-simulation algorithms generally do **not** use a known closed-form spectral
  decomposition, because obtaining it is the hard problem. Saying the right-hand form is “actually used in
  Hamiltonian-simulation arguments” can mislead; it is used for analysis/conceptual definition, while algorithms
  approximate evolution without diagonalizing.
- **Normality:** Finite-dimensional Hermitian Hamiltonians are normal; effective non-Hermitian Hamiltonians exist
  for open/conditional systems but are not physical closed-system Hamiltonians.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.7 non-normal case

- **Correctness:** Spectral failure/Jordan and SVD robustness are well summarized.
- **Nuance:** SVD does not replace functional calculus or preserve eigen-dynamics; it answers different questions.
- **Block-encoding wording:** Block encodings are unitary embeddings whose encoded block can be non-normal/
  rectangular; SVD/QSVT relevance is sound but “inside algorithms” is vague.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.8 tensor-product definition

- **Correctness:** Basis dimension and bilinearity are correct for finite-dimensional vector spaces over the same
  field.
- **Physical caveat:** Composite Hilbert spaces use tensor products for distinguishable subsystems; identical
  bosons/fermions occupy symmetric/antisymmetric subspaces and gauge constraints can prevent naive factorization.
- **Terminology:** A basis of the tensor product consists of all ordered pairs `$e_i\otimes f_j$`; implied but worth
  explicit indices.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.8 Kronecker-product example

- **Correctness:** Component order and $|01\rangle` column match the book's MSB convention.
- **Wording:** Vector tensor product is represented by a Kronecker product after bases/order are chosen; it is not
  ontologically “computed as” one independent of representation.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.8 ket abbreviations

- **Ambiguity:** `$|a\rangle|b\rangle$` can look like scalar modulus products to novices; explicit `\otimes` on
  first use per section is good.
- **Subsystem labels:** For heterogeneous registers, append A/B labels before abbreviating so order remains visible.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.8 Qiskit circuit/integer/string/statevector warning

- **Positive:** Separating four ordering concepts is unusually careful and useful.
- **Version perishability:** “Qiskit (2.x)” behavior and diagram defaults are software-version claims. Pin exact
  tested version (requirements currently 2.4.1), add a test, and date the explanation.
- **Classical-register nuance:** Printed measurement strings with multiple classical registers can include spaces
  and register-order conventions beyond simple `q_1 q_0`; this warning discusses statevector/qubits but says
  “prints a bit string” broadly.
- **Diagram nuance:** Qiskit drawers can reverse bits/configure layout; “by default” properly qualifies.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.8 quick-reference mapping

- **Potential pedagogical trap:** Both bullet lists are numerically identical, so a hurried reader may conclude
  there is no endian issue. Add a row mapping named physical labels for book factor order `(q_0,q_1)` versus
  Qiskit printed order.
- **Notation mismatch:** Header says qubits labeled `$q_0,q_1$`, but book bullet uses `$x_1,x_2$`; make the mapping
  explicit in the same row.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.8 factor-label reconciliation

- **Correctness:** If book factor 1 maps to Qiskit highest label, statevector numeric ordering coincides.
- **Overstatement:** An explicit SWAP is not always required when mapping factor 1 to `q0`; one can reinterpret/
  permute matrix axes, pass different `qargs`, reverse bits, or relabel without executing a physical SWAP gate.
  Say “apply a representation permutation (not necessarily a circuit SWAP).”
- **Developer usability:** Include a tiny executable Qiskit assertion to make this testable.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.8 inner products and general pure-state expansion

- **Correctness:** Product inner product/norm identities and computational-basis normalization are correct.
- **Wording defect:** Amplitudes are not values one can “measure”; experiments estimate probabilities/phases/
  observables and tomography reconstructs amplitudes under assumptions.
- **Product normalization rationale:** Basis kets being normalized is enough; the ordering convention itself is
  unrelated to normalization.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.8 operator Kronecker form

- **Correctness:** Component action and block-matrix representation are correct.
- **Dimension labels:** The displayed ellipsis matrix assumes entries of A are scalars and B common block; state
  dimensions for readers checking shapes.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.8 X⊗I and I⊗X examples

- **Correctness:** Both matrices and basis actions match book ordering.
- **Qiskit qualification:** `X` on `q0` corresponds to `$I\otimes X$` only under the exact factor-label mapping just
  chosen; transpilation/layout and custom register order can change physical labeling.
- **Positive:** Side-by-side matrices are high-value debugging material.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.8 tensor identities

- **Shape caveat:** Product identity requires compatible dimensions; trace/determinant formulas require square
  matrices. The determinant bullet states square shapes; trace bullet could too.
- **Correctness:** Formulas are correct.
- **Entertainment: 2/5.**

#### §4.8 pure product/separable definitions

- **Normalization wording:** Any nonzero factorization can be rescaled; “up to normalization/global phase” is
  physically oriented but mathematically product property needs neither if state already normalized.
- **Positive:** Mixed-state separability distinction is explicitly and correctly reserved.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.8 measure-zero claim

- **Measure precision:** Pure physical states are rays/projective space, while the claim invokes unit sphere;
  global-phase redundancy does not change measure-zero status, but “Haar-induced measure on projective pure states”
  is exact.
- **Interpretive caution:** Almost every Haar-random state being entangled does not mean physically reachable/
  low-depth/local states are typically Haar-random or useful.
- **“Richer” wording:** Entanglement abundance is geometric, not automatically computational resource value.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.8 Bell rank sanity check

- **Correctness:** Coefficient-matrix rank test is correct; product pure state has rank exactly one when nonzero,
  not merely “at most” one (at most includes zero matrix).
- **Pedagogy:** This effectively foreshadows Schmidt rank/SVD.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.8 Schmidt preview

- **Cross-reference defect:** It says Schmidt decomposition §4.9, but §4.9 is SVD and may derive Schmidt; exact H3
  absence means anchor/reference should be explicit.
- **Normalization:** For normalized state, $\sum_i s_i^2=1`; worth including.
- **Dimension:** Sum has at most $\min(\dim V,\dim W)` terms.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.8 partial-trace definition

- **Correctness:** Product definition plus linear extension uniquely defines finite-dimensional partial trace.
- **Basis-independent characterization:** Add
  `tr[(X⊗I)ρ]=tr[X tr_W ρ]` to explain why it is the local predictive state.
- **Wording:** The partial trace extracts an operator/reduced state, not the subsystem's ontic “state” independent
  of embedding/operational context.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.8 Bell reduced-state example

- **Correctness:** Result is $I/2`; cross terms vanish under tracing second qubit.
- **Pedagogy:** Showing one cross term, e.g. `tr_2(|00><11|)=0`, would make the operation calculational rather than
  magical.
- **Overstatement:** Reduced mixedness indicates entanglement for a **global pure state**; for global mixed states,
  mixed marginals do not diagnose entanglement.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §4.8 takeaway

- **Overclaim:** Tensor products allow correlations and entanglement, but not every multi-qubit state/resource is
  computationally useful.
- **Style:** Strong transition.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.9 SVD statement

- **Correctness:** Full SVD dimensions, ordering, nonnegativity, and `$A^\dagger A$` relation are correct.
- **Wording:** A rectangular diagonal has exactly `min(m,n)` diagonal positions; “zeros padding ... as needed” can
  imply extra singular values beyond that count. Say zero singular values are included among them and off-diagonal/
  unmatched rows/columns are zero.
- **Economy:** Mention reduced/thin SVD because it is the implementation form experienced developers most often use.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.9 invariant/stretching interpretation

- **Precision:** Singular values are invariant under left/right unitary multiplication, not arbitrary independent
  “changes of basis” if domain/codomain inner products or coordinate meanings change.
- **Stretching:** Principal axes are right singular vectors and outputs left singular vectors; one sentence would
  make the geometry concrete.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.9 operator and trace norms

- **Correctness:** Norm formulas are correct.
- **Trace-distance theorem:** Maximum total-variation distance over measurements is correct for optimized POVM;
  equal-prior success is `$p_{\rm succ}=(1+D)/2$`, which should be written rather than vaguely “determines.”
- **Notation collision:** Density matrix named `\sigma` immediately after singular values `\sigma_i`; conventions
  acknowledge this collision but local cognitive load is real.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.9 condition number

- **Technical defect:** $\kappa=\sigma_{\max}/\sigma_{\min}` for square invertible matrices is the 2-norm condition
  number; condition numbers are norm- and problem-dependent.
- **“Ill-posed”:** Large finite condition number means ill-conditioned/sensitive, not mathematically ill-posed.
- **Extension:** Rectangular/full-rank pseudoinverse condition numbers matter too.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.9 Schmidt decomposition

- **Correctness:** Form, normalization, rank/product criterion, and Bell example are correct.
- **Dimension:** Sum range and number of coefficients should be bounded by `min(dim V,dim W)`.
- **Entanglement measures:** For bipartite pure states, entropy is computed from `$s_i^2$`; name squared Schmidt
  coefficients as reduced-state eigenvalues.
- **“Maximally”:** Correct relative to two-qubit bipartition.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §4.9 QSVT preview

- **Terminology nuance:** QSP transforms phases/eigenphases in a primitive formulation; QSVT transforms singular
  values of a block-encoded matrix. Grouping them is useful but “SVD is spectrum these techniques actually
  transform” overgeneralizes QSP/qubitization.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.9 HHL preview

- **Positive:** This version carefully lists state preparation, access, precision, conditioning, and output
  extraction—much better than Chapter 1.
- **Implementation wording:** Still say “prepares a state proportional to” rather than “implements a map,” because
  normalization/nonunitarity/success amplitude matter.
- **Technical nuance:** For positive Hermitian A, singular/eigenvectors align and singular values equal eigenvalues;
  signed case needs phase/sign handling.
- **Non-Hermitian inverse:** QSVT mapping between left/right subspaces is subtle and not simply deterministic
  `σ→1/σ`; qualification as preview helps.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.9 normal-matrix SVD construction

- **Potential formula issue:** With `$A=W\Lambda W^\dagger`, `V=W`, `U=WD``, one obtains
  `$U\Sigma V^\dagger=WD|\Lambda|W^\dagger=W\Lambda W^\dagger$`; correct for
  `$D_{ii}=\lambda_i/|\lambda_i|`.
- **Kernel:** Arbitrary unit phases yield a full unitary and multiply zero singular values harmlessly.
- **“Strictly more general”:** SVD applies more broadly but does not subsume eigenvalue phase/Jordan/dynamics
  information; say broader applicability, not more informative generalization.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.9 polar decomposition

- **Correctness:** Square invertible and rank-deficient statements are correct.
- **Analogy edge case:** `$z=e^{i\theta}|z|` fails to give unique phase at $z=0`, exactly paralleling kernel
  nonuniqueness; point this out for a satisfying connection.
- **Left/right polar forms:** Only right polar decomposition shown; adequate.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.9 sanity check

- **Correctness:** Singular values `(1,1)`, `(1,1)`, `(1,0)` are correct.
- **Wording defect:** The final sentence says this distinguishes unitary/Hermitian/projector “properties,” but
  singular values alone cannot distinguish X from S or Hermitian from non-Hermitian; they only distinguish the
  projector from the two unitaries. Explicitly say singular values do **not** distinguish X and S.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.10 basis transformation formulas

- **Correctness:** With columns of U equal to f-basis vectors in e-coordinates, `$v_f=U^\dagger v_e$` and
  `$A_f=U^\dagger A_eU$` are correct.
- **Scope:** Orthonormal bases are essential; general basis changes use inverse, not necessarily adjoint.
- **Index notation:** `$f_j=\sum_iU_{ij}e_i$` clearly fixes convention and is excellent.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.10 measurement-basis implementation

- **Correctness:** Apply `$U^\dagger` then Z-basis measurement for rank-one projective measurement in columns of U.
- **Degeneracy/generalization:** This does not cover POVMs or degenerate PVM instruments.
- **Wording:** After H, amplitudes in the Hadamard basis become computational-basis amplitudes; saying a
  “computational-basis superposition” is correct but can obscure that measurement now samples `|a|²,|b|²`.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.10 active/passive distinction

- **Positive:** One of the chapter's best developer-facing explanations.
- **Subtlety:** Passive coordinate change can be viewed with basis vectors changing by U and coordinates by
  `$U^\dagger`; active operation keeps coordinates/basis fixed. The paragraph captures this.
- **Hadamard example:** “Same matrix because H=H†” is correct, though applying H to the abstract state is not the
  same map as merely representing it; clearly noted.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §4.10 diagonalization and invariants

- **Overstatement:** Hard computation becoming easy in another basis is an important pattern, but finding/
  implementing that basis may be the whole hard problem.
- **Invariant nuance:** Vector 2-norm is basis-independent only under orthonormal/unitary coordinate changes;
  determinant/trace/eigenvalues/rank are similarity invariants for endomorphisms.
- **Trace/nuclear/HS norms:** Unitarily invariant under left/right unitary transformations as appropriate; “basis-
  independent” is fine in Hilbert-space coordinates.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.11 finite Hilbert spaces

- **Correctness:** Finite-dimensional inner-product spaces are complete.
- **Terminology:** “Standard inner product” is basis-dependent wording; an abstract Hilbert space has a specified
  inner product, represented standardly in an orthonormal basis.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.11 completeness definition

- **Positive:** Cauchy/rationals analogy is concise and accurate.
- **Precision:** “Members eventually stay arbitrarily close” should quantify pairwise tail distances, not
  closeness to an unstated point.
- **Finite-dimensional proof:** Completeness assumes scalar field C is complete; implicit.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.11 infinite-dimensional contexts

- **Continuous-variable nuance:** One mode is often represented as `$L^2(\mathbb R)` and equivalently Fock space;
  multiple modes as tensor products/Fock spaces. `$L^2(\mathbb R^n)` is valid for n canonical coordinates.
- **Fock-space nuance:** Variable particle-number field modes differ from a fixed number of oscillator modes.
- **Omission:** Photonic hardware and ordinary nonrelativistic wavefunctions appear before Chapters 28/32.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.11 closing scope

- **Positive:** Includes qudits, ancillas, and truncations, preventing qubit-only tunnel vision.
- **Dimension notation:** `$d` versus `$2^n` is clear.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.12 “exactly four objects” opening

- **Major taxonomy defect:** There are not “exactly four objects” in Dirac notation. Bras/kets/inner/outer products
  are four core constructions, but matrix elements, operators, tensor products, projectors, continuous kets,
  dual maps, and distributions also appear.
- **Ket-as-column overstatement:** A ket is an abstract vector; it becomes a column after choosing a basis. The
  section itself corrects this at the end, so the opening should say “represented as.”
- **Label overstatement:** Ket contents can be algebraic expressions, eigenvalues, continuous coordinates, or
  composite labels, not merely variable names.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.12 bra/inner/outer product definitions

- **Correctness:** Shapes, conjugation, entries, rank, adjoint, and normalized-projector condition are correct.
- **Scalar shape:** An inner product is a scalar, not intrinsically a `1×1` matrix; shape analogy is useful for
  code but mathematically distinguish them.
- **Zero vectors:** Rank-one qualification correctly says both nonzero.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.12 matrix elements and shape checking

- **Positive:** Static type-checking analogy is excellent.
- **Limitation:** Abstract operators can map different spaces, partial inner products change subsystem shapes, and
  continuous bases add distributions; the simple `n×n` grammar is the core, not universal.
- **Reading direction:** Earlier section said read operator sandwiches right-to-left; shape multiplication is
  written left-to-right. Both are compatible but explicitly distinguish action order.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §4.12 worked |0〉/|+〉 example

- **Correctness:** Inner product and projector matrices are correct.
- **Terminology:** “Amplitude along |0〉” is intuitive; more precisely expansion coefficient/projection amplitude.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.12 anti-linearity

- **Correctness:** Bra formation is conjugate-linear.
- **Terminology:** The Riesz map from ket to bra is anti-linear under physics convention; useful to name because
  the dual space itself consists of linear functionals.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.12 computational-basis ordering

- **Repetition:** Ordering warning has now appeared in §4.2 and extensively §4.8; controlled repetition helps lookup
  but this version could link rather than restate generic “opposite ordering.”
- **Tensor symbol:** Again omits `\otimes`; acceptable after §4.8.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.12 resolution of identity

- **Scope:** Sum assumes a complete orthonormal finite basis; stated context but say it.
- **Correctness:** Expansion coefficient order matches physics convention.
- **“Any expression”:** Inserting identity expands compatible indices/spaces; not literally any location without
  checking domains.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.12 rank-one spectral shorthand

- **Correctness:** One term per orthonormal eigenvector repeats eigenvalues across degeneracy and is valid.
- **Index ambiguity:** Earlier spectral decomposition indexed distinct eigenvalues/projectors; say this index runs
  eigenvectors with multiplicity to avoid apparent contradiction.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.12 vector-versus-coordinates coda

- **Major self-contradiction:** The section first asserts ket *is* a column and label has no mathematics, then admits
  careful distinction. Put the accurate abstract-vector statement first and call column equality a fixed-basis
  representation.
- **Serialization analogy:** Excellent for developers, though byte formats can change semantic endianness whereas
  basis coordinate changes are linear representations.
- **Global scope:** Computational basis is not fixed for every ket in the book—X/Y bases, eigenstates, CV states,
  logical/code bases. “Default coordinate basis” is safer.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §4.13 DFT definition

- **Correctness:** Symmetric normalization and sign-paired inverse are correct.
- **Indexing:** Zero-based indexing is explicit.
- **Notation:** Hat notation is conventional; define whether vectors are columns and F acts leftward, consistent
  with earlier matrix convention.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.13 sign convention and Qiskit mapping

- **Positive:** Naming the sign convention is excellent defensive documentation.
- **Current-software risk:** The assertion about Qiskit 2.x `QFTGate` must be tested against installed 2.4.1 and
  generated circuit/matrix, not trusted from prose. It is highly sensitive to matrix, circuit, and swap ordering.
- **Potential overstatement:** Translating opposite sign by “taking the adjoint” is mathematically correct, but
  controlled-phase/readout changes are not always a blanket conjugation when algorithm definitions also change.
- **Deprecated API detail:** Appropriate for a tooling chapter, but perishable clutter in math foundations; move
  implementation status to Chapter 23/26 and keep a tested link.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.13 three-part translation checklist

- **Positive:** Sign/swaps/labels separated cleanly.
- **Wording defect:** “Every controlled-phase angle and every readout formula needs to be conjugated” is too
  categorical; some symmetric angles, relabelings, or paired forward/inverse changes cancel. Say re-derived under
  the chosen convention.
- **“Off-by-a-conjugate”:** Memorable and apt.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §4.13 key properties

- **Correctness:** Unitarity, circular convolution, and shift/phase duality are correct.
- **Insufficient precision:** Convolution theorem is precisely where symmetric normalization factors confuse
  readers; write the exact factor under this convention rather than defer.
- **Shift sign:** State one formula because direction depends on sign convention.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.13 FFT versus QFT complexity

- **Major comparison caveat:** FFT `$O(N\log N)` arithmetic operations and QFT `$O((\log N)^2)` gates act on
  fundamentally different input/output representations; the later bottleneck warning helps, but lead with this.
- **Exact gate count:** Standard exact QFT uses `$O(n^2)` controlled rotations plus swaps in an ideal gate set that
  includes arbitrary small rotations. Over a fixed finite fault-tolerant gate set, exact implementation may be
  impossible and synthesis/precision costs enter.
- **Approximate QFT:** Bounds depend on norm/success task and retained rotation range; “reduce further” needs a
  representative complexity.
- **Chapter references:** Verify §§14.5–14.6 against current headings.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.13 N=4 sanity check

- **Correctness:** With $\omega=i` and negative exponent, coefficients `(1,-i,-1,i)/2` are correct.
- **Qiskit claim:** Again should be an automated matrix assertion because swaps can make output look permuted, not
  merely conjugated.
- **Depth qualification:** Excellent distinction between count and depth.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.13 input/output bottlenecks

- **Input lower bound nuance:** Generic explicit amplitude loading costs $\Omega(N)` under standard models, but QRAM/
  oracle/data-structure access changes accounting and state preparation can exploit structure.
- **Output wording:** Measurement samples probabilities, not “samples drawn from amplitudes.”
- **Tomography lower bound:** “No way” should be scoped to recovering arbitrary full classical vector to fixed
  accuracy; shadows/queries can estimate selected properties efficiently.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.13 structured-subroutine takeaway

- **Positive:** “Accelerates unitary, not information transfer” is among the book's best anti-hype lines.
- **Amplitude estimation:** Standard amplitude estimation uses inverse QFT/phase estimation variants, while modern
  iterative variants may not; inclusion is fine at family level.
- **“Structured-data tool”:** QFT can act on states not originating as classical data; “structured-state/
  structured-readout tool” is more precise.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.**

#### §4.13 spectral-technique coda

- **Positive:** Explicitly denies literal descent, avoiding a false genealogy.
- **Conceptual nuance:** QSP/QSVT often transform singular values/eigenphases without classical spectral readout;
  “read out structured features” may suggest phase estimation is always involved.
- **Reference:** Check Chapter 16 terminology and current QFT section numbers.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.14 classical probability definitions

- **Correctness:** Distribution, expectation, variance, joint/marginal/conditional/independence formulas are
  correct for finite discrete variables.
- **Variance:** `$g` is treated as a random variable/function of outcomes; notation `$E[g]^2` is unambiguous but
  `$E[g(X)]` would align definitions.
- **Independence:** For zero-probability support points, factorization definition remains valid.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.14 Shannon entropy and mutual information

- **Correctness:** Bounds/extrema and mutual-information identity are correct.
- **Missing convention:** Joint/marginal entropy notation `$H(X)` versus distribution `$H(p)` changes silently.
- **Interpretation:** Mutual information measures average reduction in uncertainty and is nonnegative; say it is
  symmetric and equals KL divergence for stronger intuition.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.14 von Neumann entropy

- **Wording defect:** “Replace the diagonal-entry distribution by the spectrum” is risky: diagonal entries depend
  on basis and are not generally eigenvalues. Say replace a classical probability vector with density-operator
  eigenvalues.
- **Pure bipartite entanglement:** Entropies of either reduced state are equal; correct but only an entanglement
  measure for global pure states.
- **Operator logarithm:** Defined spectrally; point to §4.7 functional calculus.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.14 Holevo bound

- **Correctness:** Ensemble, average state, Holevo quantity, accessible-information upper bound, and pure-state
  reduction are correct.
- **Nuance:** Accessible information is a supremum over POVMs for a single ensemble use; collective measurements
  over block encodings/asymptotic capacities require care.
- **Amplitude comparison:** $\chi` bounds classical message information, not the storage/ontic content of arbitrary
  amplitudes; the warning is useful but should avoid implying amplitudes encode freely chosen classical bits.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.14 sampling and Bernoulli estimation

- **False universal:** “Whatever you report is an empirical frequency” excludes expectation estimators,
  likelihood/model-based inference, phase-estimation outputs, shadow estimators, and deterministic postprocessing.
  They ultimately use samples, but reports need not be frequencies.
- **Shot bound:** $\Theta(1/\epsilon^2)` for worst-case Bernoulli mean at constant confidence is correct under iid
  direct sampling.
- **Confidence factor:** More precisely `$O(\epsilon^{-2}\log(1/\delta))`; lower-bound regimes/constant factors
  depend on confidence range.
- **Noise:** Real hardware shots may not be iid due to drift/correlations, making standard concentration invalid
  without diagnostics.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.14 amplitude-estimation preview

- **Access assumptions:** Needs coherent state-preparation unitary/inverse, success marking/reflections or variants,
  and query model; “stronger” should list them.
- **Chapter reference defect:** Amplitude estimation appears more likely in Chapter 15 than Chapter 14 per TOC;
  verify exact section.
- **Part X:** Practical access/era placement is plausible.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.15 traps 1–3

- **Correctness:** Amplitude squaring, global/relative phase, and conjugation warnings are good.
- **Trap 2 nuance:** Relative phase is observable only relative to a coherent reference/through a suitable
  measurement, not absolutely.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.15 traps 4–6

- **Endian wording defect:** Frameworks do not necessarily make “the opposite choice”; they maintain several
  independent order conventions as §4.8 carefully explains.
- **QFT and Hilbert-space warnings:** Strong and accurate after model qualifications.
- **Interference:** Again presented as universal resource; replace with structure/coherent transformations/
  task-specific readout.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.15 traps 7–9

- **Positive:** Trap 7 explicitly repairs earlier measurement-only nonunitarity.
- **Eigen/singular wording:** They coincide for PSD Hermitian operators including multiplicity; agree in modulus for
  normal square matrices, correct.
- **Operator classes:** Strong compact review.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.15 traps 10–11

- **Active/passive:** Excellent.
- **Measurement-probability nuance:** Probabilities are properties of state plus measurement/instrument; for
  mixed states and POVMs this pair is `(\rho,{E_i})`, not state vector alone.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.15 traps 12–13

- **SWAP defect:** A representation mismatch needs a permutation/reindexing, not necessarily a physically executed
  SWAP. This repeats the §4.8 overstatement.
- **Ket abstraction:** Correct and contradicts §4.12's opening “ket is a column”; resolve rather than list both.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.16 renderer rationale

- **Positive:** Explains why a seemingly less scannable bullet list is intentional.
- **Maintenance:** The renderer-bug link is relative and likely correct; ensure generated book rewrites it properly.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.16 inner-product/kets/pure-state conventions

- **Correctness:** Compact and accurate.
- **Ray wording:** Pure physical states are rays under any nonzero scalar before normalization, then global phase;
  shorthand is fine.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.16 ordering and Qiskit conventions

- **Major correction:** “Or insert a SWAP” should be “or consistently permute/relabel tensor axes; a physical SWAP
  is only one implementation option.”
- **Perishability:** QFTGate sign and Qiskit mapping need automated tests pinned to Qiskit 2.4.1.
- **Tensor notation:** Uses thin-space doubled escape between kets instead of explicit `\otimes`; may visually imply
  multiplication.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.16 physics conventions

- **Hamiltonian units:** Setting $\hbar=1` requires consistent time/energy units; restoring units should be
  explained.
- **Observables/POVM wording:** POVM effects correspond to generalized measurement outcomes; not all POVMs define
  “observables” in the same Hermitian-operator value assignment.
- **Projectors:** Correct local convention.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.16 norm list

- **Consistency defect:** Hilbert–Schmidt norm is used/defined in §4.4 but omitted from “five distinct roles”; the
  list actually contains five only by excluding it. Either call it selected roles or include six.
- **Trace norm:** Good.
- **Operator absolute value:** Clear.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.**

#### §4.16 closing debugging line

- **Positive:** Concise, memorable, actionable.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### §4.17 bridge mappings

- **Pure state:** Normalized rays phrasing is slightly off—rays do not have norm; normalized vectors modulo global
  phase represent rays.
- **Measurement:** Hermitian observable plus projectors describes PVM outcomes; conditional instrument/update needs
  extra postulate.
- **Open systems:** Density matrices alone describe states; channels describe open-system behavior. The bullet
  assigns open behavior to density matrices too broadly.
- **Positive:** Clean formalism-to-physics handoff.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**

#### Footer navigation and Chapter 4 verdict

- **Navigation:** Correct.
- **Overall:** Strong, unusually careful mathematical refresher with excellent developer analogies and explicit
  convention management. Its main defects are internal ordering/cross-reference drift, occasional universalization
  of representation-specific claims, and contradictions between precise later caveats and simplified earlier
  definitions.
- **Strong-defect shortlist:** HHL described as implementing an inverse; spectral closed form implied to be the
  practical simulation method; Qiskit mismatch prescribed as physical SWAP; SVD sanity check claims singular values
  distinguish Hermiticity; ket asserted to be a column then corrected; QFT finite-gate “exact” complexity caveat;
  sampling reports reduced to frequencies; HS norm omitted from exhaustive convention list; and several likely
  wrong chapter/section links.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.**
- **Second-pass status:** Complete for this file; every numbered section, H3 subsection, local sanity check, and
  footer were read separately.

### `book/part-02-formalism/05-postulates.md` — second pass

#### Chapter 5 title, status, navigation, opening contract, and reading guide

- **Structure:** The opening efficiently converts Chapter 4’s mathematical vocabulary into a promise of operational
  rules. The three framing points and the reading guide make the chapter’s dependency role unusually legible.
- **Scope wording:** Calling the initial picture “projective measurement only” is fair as a pedagogical restriction,
  but the text should distinguish a simplified postulate set from a claim about the uniquely fundamental formulation
  of quantum theory. General measurements can be presented directly rather than only as a later correction.
