  classical simulation tractability also requires efficiently describable/applied operations. Space alone does not
  establish time efficiency.
- **DQC1 wording:** “Advantage” in DQC1 should identify the problem and evidence/complexity assumption, not sound like
  an unconditional separation. “Vanishing entanglement” depends on bipartition and measure; discord/other
  nonclassical correlations motivate the model precisely because the resource diagnosis is subtle.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The resource debate adds intellectual tension, though the correlation claim needs
  substantial narrowing.

#### §7.5 Bell-state definitions and basis

- **Correctness:** The four displayed states are normalized, mutually orthogonal, maximally entangled, and form a
  basis of the two-qubit space.
- **Definition opportunity:** “Maximally entangled” has not yet been quantified. State the operational criterion:
  either one-qubit marginal is $I/2$, equivalently both Schmidt coefficients are $1/\sqrt2$.
- **Expansion claim:** Any pure vector can be expanded in the Bell basis; any density operator requires an operator-
  basis expansion and is not generally a probabilistic mixture of Bell states. “Any two-qubit state” is ambiguous
  after Chapter 5 introduced mixed states.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The compact Bell zoo is inherently memorable.

#### §7.5 preparation circuit and singlet symmetry

- **Strong mapping defect:** With $H$ on the first qubit followed by first-controlled CNOT,
  `$|01\rangle\mapsto|\Psi^+\rangle$` and `$|10\rangle\mapsto|\Phi^-\rangle$`; the prose says the “other three” listed
  in order arise from `$|01\rangle,|10\rangle,|11\rangle$ respectively`, swapping the first two mappings. The correct
  input order for `$\Phi^-,\Psi^+,\Psi^-$` is `$|10\rangle,|01\rangle,|11\rangle$`.
- **“Up to relative signs” ambiguity:** A relative sign distinguishes Bell states and is not disposable; only overall
  global phase is irrelevant. State each mapping explicitly.
- **Singlet symmetry:** For `$U\in U(2)$`,
  `$(U\otimes U)|\Psi^-\rangle=\det(U)|\Psi^-\rangle$`; for SU(2) it is exactly invariant. “Up to global phase” is
  correct but the determinant identity would make the claim transparent.
- **Terminology:** Exchange antisymmetry and collective-rotation invariance are distinct useful properties; both are
  stated well.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The circuit and singlet symmetry make the basis feel operational rather than taxonomic.

#### §7.6 GHZ and W classes

- **Conversion wording:** Being in different SLOCC classes means neither can be converted to the other with nonzero
  probability by invertible local filtering. The preceding “cannot be converted ... by LOCC” should specify
  deterministic or stochastic direction/resource assumptions; as written, the SLOCC clarification is awkwardly
  spliced across a line.
- **Reduction claims:** Tracing one GHZ qubit yields
  `$(|00\rangle\langle00|+|11\rangle\langle11|)/2$`, which is separable; tracing one W qubit yields an entangled mixed
  state. Correct.
- **Loss versus trace:** Particle loss is modeled by tracing out only when the loss carries no additional accessible
  flag/information. The intuition is standard, but identify it as the reduced-state model.
- **Use-case claim:** Calling W “useful as a memory state for distributed protocols” is vague and citation-worthy.
  Name a protocol or phrase it as robustness motivation rather than established deployment.
- **Preparation complexity:** One $H$ plus a CNOT chain prepares GHZ under suitable connectivity. W preparation’s
  “staircase” depends on available controlled rotations/connectivity; cite or preview the actual circuit.
- **SLOCC educational load:** This acronym appears before resource transformations are developed. A one-sentence
  contrast between deterministic LOCC and nonzero-success filtering would help.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Fragile GHZ versus robust W is an effective and intuitive contrast.

#### §7.7 EPR history and singlet intuition

- **Historical compression:** EPR’s criterion of reality, locality reasoning, and incompleteness conclusion are more
  nuanced than “either action at a distance or hidden variables.” This is acceptable as a bridge only with a citation
  and wording such as “motivated the later local-hidden-variable framing.”
- **Anachronism risk:** Bell’s theorem targets local hidden-variable completions, but EPR did not itself formulate the
  modern Bell-locality polytope. Keep the historical stages distinct.
- **Same-basis anticorrelation:** Correct for the singlet under a common orthonormal basis convention, owing to its
  collective-unitary invariance.
- **Classical explanation wording:** Predetermined outcomes for every possible setting, combined with locality and
  measurement independence, is the relevant Bell target—not merely predetermined computational-basis labels.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The historical puzzle is concise and naturally motivates the inequality.

#### §7.8 local classical correlation model

- **Model-definition issue:** The conventional Bell scenario uses shared hidden variable $\lambda$, measurement
  settings $x,y$, and local response distributions `$P(a|x,\lambda)P(b|y,\lambda)$`. Here $(a,b)$ are first called
  distributed labels and then $a,b$ risk being confused with outcomes; `$s,t$` are called random bits rather than
  measurement settings. Rewrite with standard notation.
- **Polytope qualification:** The local set is a polytope for finite numbers of settings and outcomes. With unspecified
  alphabets/continuous choices, the blanket phrase needs this condition.
- **Shared randomness:** Correlated labels can encode shared randomness, while local coins can be absorbed into
  $\lambda$ for convexity. Explain this to make deterministic vertices and the word “polytope” intuitive.
- **Quantum wording:** Some entangled states/measurement choices yield points outside the local polytope; entanglement
  alone does not ensure the chosen observed correlations do.
- **“Empirical signature” nuance:** A finite experimental violation requires statistical analysis and assumptions
  about loopholes; the mathematical signature is inequality violation.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The geometric-polytope preview is promising, but notation confusion blunts it.

#### §7.9 CHSH definition and classical bound

- **Terminology defect:** The displayed $S$ is a scalar expectation combination/statistic, not the CHSH operator.
  The operator is `$\mathcal B=A_0\otimes(B_0+B_1)+A_1\otimes(B_0-B_1)$`, with
  `$S=\langle\mathcal B\rangle$`.
- **Locality notation:** Products `$A_xB_y$` should be tensor products or explicitly commuting separated observables in
  the quantum case; otherwise the expression looks like sequential multiplication on one Hilbert space.
- **Classical proof assumptions:** Reducing to fixed `$a_0,a_1,b_0,b_1$` uses deterministic hidden-variable response
  assignments, with stochastic responses absorbed into shared randomness. It also assumes setting independence and
  locality. One sentence would connect §7.8’s polytope to the one-line proof.
- **Sign-convention sentence:** Moving the single minus sign among correlators can be achieved by relabeling settings
  and outcomes, but it does not merely always “change the sign” of a fixed dataset’s $S$. The bounds are invariant;
  phrase only that.
- **Source-format risk:** Doubled set braces recur.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The one-line `$b_0\pm b_1$` proof is exceptionally efficient and satisfying.

#### §7.9 Tsirelson bound and saturating settings

- **Correctness:** The selected singlet axes produce the four stated correlators and `$S=2\sqrt2$`; the sign caveat is
  correct.
- **Observable condition:** State that Alice and Bob’s observables act on separate factors (or commute across parties).
  Merely having spectra in $[-1,1]$ on a common arbitrary algebra is not the full Bell-scenario specification.
- **Proof gap:** The classical bound gets a proof, while the quantum bound is asserted. A compact identity
  `$\mathcal B^2=4I-[A_0,A_1]\otimes[B_0,B_1]$` plus norm bound would justify the chapter’s central quantitative claim,
  or explicitly defer its proof.
- **Naming/citation:** Tsirelson/Cirel’son attribution and bound should have a formal source.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The explicit axes make the violation reproducible rather than mystical.

#### §7.9 experimental interpretation and no-signalling

- **Historical sourcing:** Aspect-era and 2015 loophole-free experiments need citations, especially because “most
  tested” and the exact status of loopholes are empirical claims.
- **Assumption wording:** Loophole-free tests specifically avoid relying on the fair-sampling assumption within their
  design thresholds. Freedom-of-choice is addressed statistically/spacelike but not proven metaphysically.
  “Assumptions the experiments test” is inaccurate; experiments operate under and constrain combinations of
  assumptions.
- **Conclusion overreach:** Bell violations rule out Bell-local hidden-variable models satisfying measurement
  independence and relevant experimental assumptions, not “local hidden variables ... as a model of physical
  reality” without qualification. Contextual/nonlocal/superdeterministic alternatives are logically distinct.
- **No-signalling:** Correctly separates nonlocal correlation from communication. Marginal independence follows from
  the quantum formalism and appropriate experimental statistics; correlators require classical record comparison.
- **Terminology:** “No information travels faster than light” is the operational conclusion, not a mechanism inferred
  from collapse timing.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The no-signalling coda answers the reader’s immediate worry at exactly the right moment.

#### §7.10 Schmidt decomposition theorem

- **Correctness:** Existence, nonnegative normalized Schmidt coefficients, rank, and derivation via SVD of the
  coefficient matrix are correct in finite dimensions.
- **Uniqueness defect:** “Unique up to degeneracies” is incomplete. Even with distinct nonzero coefficients, Schmidt
  vectors are unique only up to paired phase choices; within degenerate coefficient subspaces there is correlated
  unitary freedom, and zero-coefficient basis completion is arbitrary.
- **Dimension bound:** Explicitly state `$r\le\min(\dim\mathcal H_A,\dim\mathcal H_B)$`; it makes later maximal-
  entanglement statements precise.
- **SVD convention:** Depending on whether coefficients are indexed as rows $A$/columns $B$, complex conjugation
  enters the mapping from right singular vectors to kets. A one-line derivation should protect against a silent
  transpose/conjugate error.
- **Source-format risk:** Doubled set braces recur.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The SVD connection rewards the reader for Chapter 4 and makes the theorem feel computational.

#### §7.10 product criterion, marginal spectra, and invariants

- **Correctness:** Rank-one iff product, shared nonzero marginal spectra, and invariance of Schmidt data under local
  unitaries are correct.
- **Maximal-entanglement dimension defect:** If subsystem dimensions differ, a pure state cannot make both full
  marginals `$I/d$` with one unspecified $d$. Uniform Schmidt coefficients mean maximal entanglement at rank
  `$d=\min(d_A,d_B)$`; the smaller marginal is maximally mixed and the larger is maximally mixed only on the Schmidt
  support.
- **Invariant/completeness opportunity:** For bipartite pure states, equal Schmidt-coefficient multisets are not merely
  invariants; they completely characterize local-unitary equivalence. State this useful stronger result.
- **Rank limitation:** Schmidt rank alone is a coarse entanglement witness, not a quantity that orders entanglement
  amount for same-rank states.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The two memorisable consequences are exactly the right selection.

#### §7.11 partial trace in Schmidt form

- **Correctness:** The reduced-state diagonalization and Bell marginal are correct.
- **Notation consistency:** Earlier chapters use lowercase `$\mathrm{tr}$`; this section switches to
  `$\mathrm{Tr}$`. Standardize unless the capitalization intentionally distinguishes partial trace.
- **Operational quantifier:** The reduced state predicts all local measurement statistics, but “every outcome” should
  be phrased as the probability distribution for every local measurement.
- **Repetition:** The Bell reduction was worked in detail in §§5.11–5.12. Here its value is the one-line Schmidt
  derivation; explicitly contrast the two methods rather than repeat the conclusion as new.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Schmidt coefficients turning directly into eigenvalues is a satisfying compression.

#### §7.11 local randomness, correlations, and no-signalling

- **Measurement overclaim:** `$I/2$` yields uniform probabilities for every **orthonormal-basis rank-one projective**
  measurement, not for every possible POVM/outcome scheme. A biased POVM can have unequal effect traces.
- **Fair-coin analogy:** Useful for binary projective readout, but it should not imply a pre-existing classical bit.
- **“All structure” overclaim:** The local marginal itself contains structure—here maximal symmetry and dimension.
  The information distinguishing this Bell state from other states with the same marginals lives in correlations;
  “all” is rhetorically understandable but technically excessive.
- **No-signalling condition:** Alice’s marginal is invariant under Bob’s local **trace-preserving** operation when its
  outcome is ignored. Conditional on Bob’s communicated/postselected result, Alice’s state changes. “What happens” is
  too broad without this qualification.
- **Communication wording:** Alice cannot access joint correlations from her subsystem alone; after classical
  communication and comparison she can. Correct central point.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The fair-coin/local-versus-joint contrast is vivid and well placed.

#### §7.12 teleportation resource account

- **Correctness:** Under the standard Bell-measurement bit convention, the correction table
  `$00\to I,01\to X,10\to Z,11\to ZX$` is correct; explicitly stating right-to-left order usefully prevents the common
  $XZ/ZX$ confusion (which differs only by global phase here).
- **Convention dependency:** The correction table depends on which measured bit records which qubit and on the CNOT/H
  Bell-measurement circuit. Tie the table to the figure’s wire/classical-bit order.
- **Misleading slogan:** “No qubit travelled” means the unknown message carrier did not travel from Alice to Bob
  during the protocol. A physical system had to distribute the Bell pair beforehand, and quantum information/state
  reconstruction is transferred. State this resource-accounting caveat.
- **No-cloning link:** Alice’s input state is destroyed by the Bell measurement, consistent with no-cloning; mentioning
  this would elegantly connect Chapter 5.
- **Ebit precision:** An ebit is a unit of bipartite entanglement equivalent asymptotically to a shared Bell pair, not
  parenthetically identical to “one maximally entangled pair” in every dimensional/resource setting.
- **Figure review limitation:** The Markdown reference and descriptive alt text are present. The SVG itself was not
  opened here because the mandated pass is currently confined to one manuscript file; it requires its own later asset
  review.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The correction table and explicit accounting make a famous protocol refreshingly concrete.

#### §7.12 superdense coding

- **Correctness:** With a pre-shared Bell pair, four local Pauli choices create four orthogonal Bell states, allowing
  two classical bits to be decoded after one-qubit transmission.
- **“Reverse roles” wording:** Dense coding is resource-dual in spirit, not simply teleportation with Alice/Bob roles
  reversed. “Complementary protocol” is more accurate.
- **Capacity caveat:** The two-bit statement assumes ideal noiseless transmission and pre-shared maximal entanglement;
  it does not violate the unassisted classical capacity because the ebit is a consumed/pre-established resource.
- **Operator order:** $ZX$ versus $XZ$ changes only global phase on the encoded Bell state, but maintaining one
  convention is good.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Extremely compact and still operationally complete.

#### §7.12 Ekert protocol summary

- **Security overclaim:** A CHSH value “close to `$2\sqrt2$`” does not simply prove pairs were undisturbed, nor does any
  eavesdropper necessarily push it below an unspecified threshold in a binary manner. Noise, finite statistics,
  imperfect devices, reconciliation, privacy amplification, and the security model determine the key rate.
- **Protocol conflation:** Original E91 and device-independent CHSH-based QKD are related but not interchangeable in a
  one-paragraph description. Specify which variant is meant and which measurement rounds generate key versus test
  Bell violation.
- **Correlation sign:** Singlet outcomes are anticorrelated in matching bases and require a bit flip/convention before
  forming a shared raw key.
- **Citation need:** Ekert attribution, security relation, and threshold need formal sources.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The Bell-test-to-security link is compelling, but its certainty is disproportionate to the
  omitted protocol machinery.

#### §7.12 unifying resource claim

- **Quantification:** Resource inequalities among ebits, qubit channels, and classical communication are a powerful
  framework. Writing the two protocols symbolically would sharpen the unification.
- **Overclaim:** “Most protocols of practical interest” is far too broad across quantum computing, sensing,
  simulation, and cryptography. Restrict to canonical bipartite quantum-communication protocols.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The resource-ledger viewpoint is a satisfying synthesis.

#### §7.13 pure-state entropy of entanglement

- **Correctness:** Entanglement entropy, its Schmidt formula, product/maximal values, LOCC monotonicity, and asymptotic
  pure-state concentration/dilution reversibility are correct in the standard i.i.d. vanishing-error regime.
- **“One good measure” nuance:** Entanglement entropy is the unique asymptotic pure bipartite measure under suitable
  axioms, but Rényi entropies, Schmidt rank, geometric entanglement, and one-shot measures remain useful. State the
  operational regime that makes it essentially unique.
- **Rate wording:** Distillable entanglement is Bell pairs **per input copy**; entanglement cost is Bell pairs **per
  prepared output copy**. “The rate at which Bell pairs are required” should specify reciprocal/resource units to
  avoid dimensional ambiguity.
- **LOCC monotonicity:** On average under stochastic LOCC is the relevant pure-state statement; individual successful
  branches may be more entangled.
- **Notation:** Introducing von Neumann entropy only by its diagonal Schmidt formula is adequate locally, but `$S$`
  collides with the CHSH statistic in the immediately prior section. Use `$S_{\rm vN}$` briefly.
- **Citation need:** The asymptotic reversibility theorem deserves attribution.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The ebit calibration gives the entropy formula an immediate operational meaning.

#### §7.13 mixed-state and multipartite measures

- **Definition scope:** Entanglement of formation is a natural convex-roof measure, not **the** natural definition of
  mixed-state entanglement. The state is entangled by nonseparability; a measure quantifies a chosen conversion cost.
- **Asymptotic nuance:** Regularized entanglement of formation equals entanglement cost; single-copy $E_F$ need not
  itself be the asymptotic preparation rate. The preceding resource discussion makes this distinction important.
- **Bound entanglement:** Positive entanglement with zero distillable entanglement is correct; “positive entanglement
  of formation” follows for entangled finite-dimensional states but needs the chosen measure context.
- **Negativity:** The formula is correct. Nonzero negativity is sufficient but not necessary for entanglement in
  general; for $2\times2$ and $2\times3$, PPT is necessary and sufficient—an excellent useful caveat.
- **Bell violation as measure:** Violation of a particular inequality is task/scenario dependent and is not generally
  an LOCC entanglement measure. The closing list says choose a “measure” matching the resource, then includes fidelity
  and Bell violation; call these witnesses/figures of merit rather than measures.
- **Multipartite scope:** GHZ/W SLOCC inequivalence correctly motivates the lack of a single scalar order.
- **Render risk:** Negativity’s trace-norm delimiters are doubled.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Bound entanglement is an intriguing complication, though the rapid measure proliferation may
  overwhelm without a comparison table.

#### §7.14 bridge to gates

- **Static/dynamic simplification:** Chapters 5–7 already discussed unitary dynamics, Hamiltonians, rotations, and
  measurement. Calling them purely “static” is an organizational shorthand, not a literal content description.
- **Decomposition overpromise:** Exact arbitrary $n$-qubit unitary decomposition is exponentially costly and depends
  on the gate model; Chapter 8 should distinguish existence/universality from efficient synthesis.
- **Continuity:** Reusing the Bell preparation circuit as the first decomposition example creates an excellent bridge.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Clear momentum into circuit mechanics.

#### §7.14 sanity checks and footer

- **Check 2 exposes earlier defect:** The four inputs do produce the Bell basis up to global signs, but the requested
  exercise should ask readers to record the mapping; doing so reveals §7.5’s swapped `$|01\rangle/|10\rangle$`
  correspondence.
- **Check 3:** The one-qubit GHZ marginal is $I/2$, but this alone does not display the key distinction from W; asking
  for the two-qubit marginal would reinforce §7.6.
- **Check 4:** The factorization is `$|+\rangle\otimes|+\rangle$`; good quick determinant/product exercise.
- **Check 5:** Strong and appropriately tied to the chapter’s quantitative centerpiece.
- **Coverage gap:** No sanity check exercises Schmidt decomposition or an entanglement entropy, despite §§7.10 and
  7.13 being major new tools.
- **Navigation:** Footer links are syntactically consistent.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The checks are concrete and cumulative rather than perfunctory.

#### Chapter 7 overall verdict

- **Overall:** An impressively concise survey with strong sequencing from tensor products through Bell tests to
  resource theory. Its circuit mappings, one-line CHSH proof, GHZ/W contrast, and Schmidt-to-marginal connection are
  especially effective.
- **Strong-defect shortlist:** Bell-circuit inputs `$|01\rangle$` and `$|10\rangle$` are mapped to the wrong named Bell
  states by the “respectively” sentence; entanglement is repeatedly equated too broadly with correlations beyond any
  classical model; maximal Schmidt entanglement ignores unequal subsystem dimensions; every local measurement on
  `$I/2$` is said to have uniform outcomes; remote marginals are claimed insensitive to anything on the other side
  without trace-preserving/outcome-forgetting conditions; E91 security is reduced to near-maximal CHSH implying no
  disturbance; and classical simulation hardness is inferred too directly from Hilbert-space dimension/entanglement.
- **Recurring slight concerns:** Historical and theorem claims need citations; resource statements omit asymptotic,
  i.i.d., error, connectivity, and security assumptions; “state” sometimes ambiguously means pure vector after mixed
  states were introduced; LOCC/SLOCC terminology arrives too compressed; and universal quantifiers routinely outrun
  the intended circuit-model setting.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The chapter is dense but unusually lively, with a conceptual or operational payoff in nearly
  every short section.
- **Second-pass status:** Complete for this manuscript file; the opening, all fourteen numbered sections, every
  displayed proof/protocol/example, sanity-check list, and footer were read and reviewed separately. The referenced
  SVG remains queued for its own asset-file pass.

### `book/part-04-gates-and-circuits/08-quantum-gates.md` — second pass

#### Chapter 8 title, status, navigation, opening contract, and reading guide

- **Static/dynamic repetition:** Chapters 5–7 were not purely static: they included Schrödinger evolution, Bloch
  rotations, measurement dynamics, and several circuits. Present Chapter 8 as the systematic gate **catalog and
  synthesis** rather than the first dynamics.
- **Gate arity contradiction:** The opening says gates act on one or two qubits at a time, while §8.1 correctly defines
  an $n$-qubit gate and later sections cover multi-controlled gates. Say elementary/native circuit libraries are often
  one- and two-qubit.
- **Realizability overclaim:** The chapter concerns ideal unitaries the model specifies and operations hardware
  approximates under noise/control constraints. “Actually realise” promises exact physical gates too strongly.
- **Arbitrary-unitary scope:** Compiling a generic $n$-qubit unitary is exponentially costly; foreground structured
  target unitaries so universality is not mistaken for efficiency.
- **Part references:** Verify Parts 6, 8, and 9 against the current book map. The Chapter 7 review already found drift
  risk.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The practical questions form a crisp agenda; inviting the cheat sheet alongside is helpful.

#### §8.1 unitary-gate definition

- **Correctness:** U($2^n$), inverse, norm preservation, and linearity are correct for ideal closed-system gates.
- **Abstraction distinction:** “Any element” of U($2^n$) is a valid abstract gate, but not necessarily a directly
  available elementary operation or efficiently synthesizable circuit. Retain this distinction throughout.
- **Information wording:** “Losing nothing” applies to the closed modeled register. Leakage, discarded ancillas,
  measurement, and noise make effective operations nonunitary.
- **Classical comparison:** Classical AND/OR are irreversible as two-input/one-output Boolean functions, but reversible
  classical embeddings exist with retained inputs and ancillas. “No quantum analogue” is misleading because coherent
  reversible implementations compute these functions; the text immediately describes one.
- **Toffoli detail:** Toffoli maps `$(a,b,c)\mapsto(a,b,c\oplus ab)$`; with $c=0$ the target contains AND while inputs
  remain. Writing this would turn the caveat into a useful preview.
- **Parallelism discipline:** Explicitly denying that linear action alone supplies speedup is excellent.
- **Cross-reference suspicion:** Verify §§10.2 and 13.1; interference may not be the only resource framing used later.
- **Render risk:** Doubled norm escapes recur.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The three-consequence rhythm works, and the parallelism correction earns trust.

#### §8.1 Hermitian generators and hardware

- **Correctness:** Hermitian exponentials are unitary, and every finite-dimensional unitary has Hermitian logarithms up
  to a phase/branch choice.
- **Physical-generator defect:** A mathematical logarithm of a gate is not necessarily “the Hamiltonian one would
  engineer.” Hardware may realize a time-dependent pulse sequence, work in a rotating frame, compile through other
  gates, or be unable to implement that generator directly. Say it is **a possible ideal generator** if physically
  accessible.
- **Nonuniqueness:** Generator eigenvalues are defined modulo `$2\pi/t$`; $t=0$ is exceptional. “Always” needs real
  nonzero duration and a branch choice.
- **Notation collision:** The next section acknowledges $H$ overload only for Hadamard, but here $H$ also competes with
  the standard gate throughout. Using $K$ for a generic generator would reduce friction.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Concise bridge to physics, weakened by identifying algebraic existence with engineering.

#### §8.2 Pauli algebra

- **Correctness:** Matrices, Hermiticity, unitarity, involution, pairwise anticommutation, and real spanning of
  traceless Hermitian `$2\times2$` matrices are correct.
- **Logical connection:** The full Hermitian basis is `$\{I,X,Y,Z\}$`; therefore
  `$H=h_0I+\vec h\cdot\vec\sigma$`. The text says Paulis span only the traceless part and then correctly adds $I$,
  but one explicit sentence would clarify.
- **Unitary parameterization:** `$e^{-i\alpha}R_{\hat n}(\theta)$` is correct up to nonunique axes/angles and sign
  conventions. Define `$R_{\hat n}$` before using it or point forward explicitly.
- **Source-format risk:** Doubled anticommutator set braces recur.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Compact and reference-friendly.

#### §8.2 operational meanings and downstream roles

- **Correctness:** $X$ swaps basis states, $Z$ applies a relative sign, `$Y=iXZ$`, and Pauli gates induce half-turns up
  to global phase.
- **Order convention:** Because `$XZ=-ZX$`, the identity `$Y=iXZ$` is convention-sensitive; noting `$Y=-iZX$` prevents
  order errors.
- **“Quantum NOT”:** Useful nickname, but $X$ is NOT only in the computational basis; unlike classical NOT, it acts
  coherently on arbitrary states.
- **Pauli group definition gap:** Mention phases `$\{\pm1,\pm i\}$` and tensor products when claiming it underpins
  stabilizers; otherwise “generate” is underspecified.
- **Part-reference risk:** Verify error correction is indeed Part 8.
- **Asset review limitation:** `figures/pauli-x.svg` is referenced with useful alt text but remains queued for its own
  one-file asset pass.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Names and geometric actions keep the matrix list tangible.

#### §8.3 Hadamard action and algorithmic role

- **Correctness:** Matrix, basis maps, involution, and uniform-superposition identity are correct.
- **Subjective superlative:** “Single most important” is harmless enthusiasm but invites pointless ranking; “one of the
  most ubiquitous” is defensible.
- **Algorithm-history compression:** These algorithms commonly begin relevant registers with Hadamards, but their
  advantage does not come from uniform superposition alone. The prior section’s warning should be echoed here.
- **Order-finding wording:** Shor’s order finding often uses Hadamards/QFT preparation on a control register, subject to
  implementation variants. Fine at this level.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The recurrence across landmark algorithms gives the gate immediate stature.

#### §8.3 conjugation versus measurement basis change

- **Correctness:** `$HXH=Z$`, `$HZH=X$`, and applying one $H$ before $Z$ readout implements an $X$-basis measurement.
- **Explanation defect:** The missing second $H$ is not fundamentally because “measurement consumes the rotated
  state”; ideal projective measurement need not physically destroy the system. It is because measuring $Z$ after $H$
  has effects `$H^\dagger P_zH$`, so the adjoint is already part of the probability/operator transformation rather
  than a second circuit gate.
- **Outcome mapping:** Computational 0/1 correspond to $X$ eigenvalues +1/−1 respectively; worth making explicit rather
  than calling it generic bookkeeping.
- **Cross-reference risk:** Verify §11.7.
- **Asset review limitation:** `figures/hadamard.svg` has descriptive alt text but remains queued for its own asset
  pass.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Separating conjugation from the measurement recipe addresses a genuinely common confusion.

#### §8.4 S, T, and general phase gates

- **Correctness:** The matrices and relations `$S^2=Z$`, `$T^4=Z$`, `$S=P(\pi/2)$`, and `$T=P(\pi/4)$` are correct
  under the selected root branches.
- **Root nonuniqueness:** Matrix roots are not unique. Writing `$S=\sqrt Z$` and `$T=\sqrt[4]Z$` names conventional
  roots, not uniquely determined operations.
- **Naming confusion:** Explain why $T$ is called the “$\pi/8$ gate” despite placing phase $\pi/4$ on $|1\rangle$:
  up to global phase it is `$R_Z(\pi/4)$`, whose exponent contains $\pi/8$.
- **Capitalization consistency:** Later prose uses “T-count”; mathematical/gate-style writing often uses `$T$-count`.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The phase ladder is simple and easy to retain.

#### §8.4 universality and fault-tolerant cost

- **Clifford-simulation qualification:** Clifford circuits are efficiently classically simulable under stabilizer
  inputs/measurements (Gottesman–Knill conditions), not because the abstract Clifford group alone makes every process
  involving it simulable.
- **Universality wording:** `$\{H,T,\mathrm{CNOT}\}$` is approximately universal; arbitrary continuous unitaries
  generally require approximation. State this before Solovay–Kitaev.
- **Fault-tolerance overstatement:** In many surface-code architectures, logical Clifford gates are cheaper than
  non-Clifford $T$ gates, but not “nearly free”; they still consume qubits, code cycles, routing, lattice-surgery
  operations, and decoding effort.
- **Magic-state condition:** Standard injected logical $T$ implementations consume a distilled magic state, but
  alternative codes/gauge fixing/transversal non-Clifford schemes alter the accounting. “Every” is architecture-
  dependent.
- **Metric opportunity:** Compilation optimizes $T$ count, $T$ depth, ancillas, Clifford overhead, and spacetime
  volume; count alone is not always dominant.
- **Cross-reference risk:** Verify Chapter 23 and §8.14.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The sudden connection from a tiny diagonal matrix to fault-tolerant cost is compelling.

#### §8.5 axis rotations and explicit matrices

- **Correctness:** Exponential definitions and displayed $R_X/R_Z$ matrices match the standard convention.
- **Completeness:** $R_Y$ is defined but uniquely omitted from the explicit matrices. Adding its real matrix would
  complete the reference value with little cost.
- **Periodicity precision:** The induced Bloch-vector SO(3) rotation is $2\pi$-periodic, while the SU(2) representative
  changes sign at $2\pi$ and repeats exactly at $4\pi$. Physical isolated-state predictions remain $2\pi$-periodic
  unless a control/reference branch converts the sign to relative phase.
- **Half-angle explanation:** It is not simply “hence” from periodicity; it follows from the SU(2)-to-SO(3) double
  cover/generator normalization. The current intuition is good if the reference caveat is retained.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Concise and geometric.

#### §8.5 Euler decomposition and hardware

- **Correctness:** U(2) phase plus ZYZ decomposition is correct; ZXZ is another valid Euler convention.
- **“Equivalently” nuance:** ZYZ and ZXZ require different reparameterized angles, not the same displayed
  `$\beta,\gamma,\delta$` in general. Use primed variables.
- **Nonuniqueness:** Euler angles are nonunique at singular cases and modulo periodicities; compilers need canonical
  ranges/tolerance.
- **Native-gate claim:** Platforms often expose calibrated equatorial rotations plus virtual $Z$, rather than both
  physical continuous $R_X$ and $R_Z$. “Native” should distinguish physical pulses from zero-duration frame updates.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The compiler connection gives the decomposition a clear purpose.

#### §8.6 CNOT definition and Bell preparation

- **Correctness:** Matrix and control-first/target-second action match the book’s basis ordering.
- **Convention:** Many SDK diagrams/orderings differ; repeat the control/target and endianness warning in the figure
  caption or Appendix A.
- **Superlative:** “Single most useful” is subjective. “Canonical” or “most widely used in textbook decompositions”
  avoids ranking native CZ/iSWAP platforms by a CNOT-centric standard.
- **Asset review limitation:** `figures/cnot.svg` has informative alt text but awaits its own file pass.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The immediate Bell link reinforces continuity.

#### §8.6 CZ, SWAP-family gates, and identities

- **Correctness:** CZ action/symmetry and the displayed CNOT decompositions of CZ and SWAP are correct under the stated
  qubit ordering.
- **Cost wording:** CZ synthesis uses one CNOT **plus two Hadamards**; “at the cost of one CNOT” is a two-qubit-cost
  accounting, not total gate count. Label the metric.
- **Native-platform specificity:** iSWAP and square-root SWAP occur in exchange-coupled platforms beyond a generic
  “some superconducting architectures”; square-root iSWAP/fSim/CZ may be the more common named native operations on
  particular devices. Avoid implying one uniform superconducting gate set.
- **Phase convention:** `$\sqrt{\mathrm{SWAP}}$` also has branch/global-phase conventions; define it later if used.
- **Assets:** `figures/cz.svg` and `figures/swap.svg` have useful alt text and remain queued for separate asset review.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The identities are exactly the sort readers will reuse.

#### §8.6 hardware cost and compilation

- **Generalization:** Two-qubit gates are usually lower fidelity/slower than one-qubit gates in current gate-model
  devices, but measurements, resets, leakage mitigation, and long-range moves can dominate particular platforms.
- **Optimization metrics:** Minimizing raw two-qubit count is one objective; depth, critical-path error, direction,
  crosstalk, calibration drift, and routing swaps can matter more. The next synthesis section should reflect this
  multi-objective reality.
- **Routing wording:** Compilers route logical interactions **through** limited hardware connectivity, often inserting
  swaps or teleportation-like moves—not “route gates around” connectivity.
- **Temporal stability:** Claims about current hardware cost are time-sensitive and need platform-specific sources or
  deliberately general wording.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Practical constraints arrive at the right point, after the algebraic identities.

#### §8.7 controlled-unitary definition and examples

- **Correctness:** The basis-conditional action, CNOT/CZ identifications, and extension to multiple all-one controls
  are correct.
- **Superposition opportunity:** Show the action on
  `$(\alpha|0\rangle+\beta|1\rangle)|\psi\rangle`; it immediately demonstrates why controlled-$U$ generally
  entangles and prevents readers imagining a classical branch test.
- **Global-phase trap:** Controlled-$U$ makes a global phase of the target operation relative between control branches.
  Thus controlled versions of phase-equivalent unitaries need not be phase-equivalent. This is an important omission
  after Chapters 5–6 discuss global phase.
- **Cross-reference defect:** Chapter 6/earlier review context says Chapter 14 concerns amplitude estimation, while this
  section sends QFT-controlled phases to Chapter 14. Verify; this looks like chapter-number drift.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Clear definition, with the entangling superposition example conspicuously absent.

#### §8.7 multi-control decomposition claims and citation

- **Source syntax defect:** `$\mathrm{C}^2(X) = $ Toffoli` closes math before “Toffoli,” producing awkward rendered
  spacing/punctuation. Put the whole relation in one expression.
- **Cost-model ambiguity:** “Elementary gates” is undefined and is compared with “Toffolis” in adjacent clauses.
  Counts differ dramatically among one-/two-qubit gates, Clifford+$T$, relative-phase Toffolis, and connectivity
  models.
- **Ancilla-count suspicion:** Linear-size exact multi-controlled-NOT constructions depend on the number/type of clean
  or dirty ancillas and chosen primitive set. The precise assertions—one clean ancilla gives `$O(k)$` Toffolis, one
  dirty ancilla gives `$O(k)$` elementary gates, none gives `$O(k^2)$`—need direct theorem/construction citations and
  constant/range qualifications; attributing all modern variants to Barenco 1995 may be historically inaccurate.
- **Terminology:** Define that a clean ancilla starts in and is restored to a known state, while a dirty ancilla starts
  arbitrary but must be restored. The parenthesis gives only half of the dirty definition.
- **Modernity:** Multi-control synthesis is active and target-gate-/ancilla-dependent; “canonical decompositions all
  trace back” is rhetorical history rather than a technical statement.
- **Citation quality:** Full reference is welcome and far better than bare name-dropping; integrate it into the
  bibliography/style system consistently.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The clean-versus-dirty tradeoff is practically interesting, though too compressed to trust
  without a cost table.

#### §8.8 Toffoli and Fredkin

- **Toffoli universality qualification:** Toffoli can realize arbitrary reversible Boolean computation when constants
  and ancillas (including initialized 1s) are allowed. Lifting an irreversible circuit also requires garbage
  management/uncomputation, not merely output wires that “absorb” information.
- **Fredkin qualification:** Fredkin is universal for **conservative** reversible computation with appropriate
  encodings/ancillas; “also classically universal” needs that resource convention because it preserves Hamming weight.
- **Permutation wording:** Every reversible classical operation is a permutation of basis strings; Fredkin is
  specifically a controlled wire swap/conservative primitive, not uniquely natural for “the underlying operation is
  a permutation.”
- **Decomposition specificity:** The six-CNOT/seven-$T$-or-$T^\dagger$/two-$H$ exact Toffoli decomposition is a standard
  ancilla-free Clifford+$T$ construction, but relative-phase, measurement-assisted, ancilla-assisted, and connectivity-
  aware variants have different costs. Label the model and whether $T^\dagger$ counts as one $T$ resource.
- **Asset review limitation:** `figures/toffoli.svg` has useful alt text but awaits its own file pass.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Strong bridge from classical logic to fault-tolerant accounting.

#### §8.9 definition and two-stage universality argument

- **Global phase/norm:** A finite set such as Clifford+$T$ is normally dense in SU($2^n$) or projective unitary space;
  approximating arbitrary U($2^n$) including every global phase requires care. Specify equivalence up to global phase
  and the norm.
- **Exact-decomposition cost:** Single-qubit gates plus CNOT exactly generate arbitrary unitaries, but generic
  decompositions require exponentially many gates. “Finite” must not suggest efficient.
- **Finite-set statement:** `$\{H,T\}$` approximates single-qubit unitaries up to phase; universality relies on density,
  inverses/closure, and approximation conventions developed next.
- **Source-format risk:** Doubled gate-set braces recur.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The continuous-then-discrete two-stage argument is pedagogically strong.

#### §8.9 examples of universal sets

- **Potentially confusing set:** `$\{H,S,\mathrm{CNOT},\mathrm{Toffoli}\}$` is redundant in presentation and its route
  to complex universality is not obvious. Since `$S$` plus real gates supplies complex phases and H+Toffoli supplies
  real computational universality, explain rather than merely list.
- **Non-Clifford theorem:** “Any single non-Clifford single-qubit gate together with Clifford generators” needs a
  theorem citation and phase/density qualification. Readers may wrongly extend it to arbitrary non-Clifford
  multi-qubit gates or noisy channels.
- **Entangling-gate theorem:** Any entangling two-qubit unitary plus arbitrary local unitaries is universal under the
  standard exact/approximate theorem, but “generic” is weaker and then parenthetically redefined as any entangler.
  Use the precise imprimitive/entangling condition and cite it.
- **H+Toffoli nuance:** Computational universality via real encoding is not the same as densely generating every
  complex unitary on the original space. The paragraph explains the extra encoded qubit well; “one-qubit overhead”
  should say one rebit/ancillary encoding dimension and state how outputs are recovered.
- **Citation formatting:** The Shi/Aharonov references are valuable; consolidate with the project bibliography.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The surprising H+Toffoli result is genuinely engaging.

#### §8.9 hardware relevance

- **Correctness:** An entangling two-qubit gate plus sufficiently controllable single-qubit rotations provides
  universality in principle.
- **Controllability caveat:** “Exposes” gates through an API does not establish arbitrary precision, coherence,
  addressability, or scalable connectivity. The practical caveat helps but could name synthesis overhead.
- **Part-reference risk:** Verify Part 9.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Useful summary, somewhat repetitive of the opening.

#### §8.10 Clifford group and Gottesman–Knill

- **Definition:** Normalizer definition and H/S/CNOT generating set are correct up to phases.
- **Theorem scope:** The stated input/measurement version is a correct restricted Gottesman–Knill case; the stronger
  stabilizer theorem allows stabilizer inputs, Pauli measurements, classical feed-forward, and related operations.
  Presenting the richer version would better support later error correction.
- **Overclaim:** Efficient classical simulation rules out a superpolynomial computational advantage for those tasks
  under usual models, but “give no quantum advantage” is too absolute across communication, sampling constants,
  hardware access, and problem formulations.
- **Fault-tolerance defect:** Clifford operations are not all transversal in “most fault-tolerant codes.” Surface codes
  commonly use lattice surgery/code deformation, and Eastin–Knill constrains transversal universality. They may be
  relatively cheaper/easier fault-tolerantly, but “map ... transversally, with minimal overhead” is materially false
  as a general explanation.
- **Chapter drift:** Earlier the reading guide points Part 8 to error correction; here it says Chapter 19. Verify both.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The simulable-yet-essential paradox is a strong organizing idea.

#### §8.10 adding T and resource accounting

- **Universality qualification:** For the standard qubit Clifford group, adjoining $T$ gives approximate universality.
  “Any one non-Clifford gate” is a broad theorem claim needing constraints/citation, especially beyond single-qubit
  unitaries.
- **T-count overreach:** $T$ count is a major proxy, not the complete “structural reason quantum advantage ... is
  captured” by one scalar. $T$ depth, stabilizer rank/magic monotones, magic-state factory throughput, Clifford
  routing, measurements, and spacetime volume all matter.
- **Magic-state absoluteness:** Standard surface-code $T$ injection consumes distilled magic states; alternative
  fault-tolerant architectures can implement non-Clifford gates differently.
- **Source-format risk:** Doubled set braces recur.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The free-versus-expensive layer model is memorable when presented as architecture-dependent.

#### §8.11 generic Solovay–Kitaev statement

- **Hypothesis wording:** A gate set must generate a dense subgroup of SU(2), be finite (in the standard statement),
  and usually include efficiently computable inverses. Calling it merely “universal” risks circularity but is
  acceptable if §8.9 defines density precisely.
- **Exponent currency:** `$c\approx3.97$` belongs to the Dawson–Nielsen construction, while improved variants have
  different exponents. “Toward 2” needs a precise cited result rather than a vague research-status claim.
- **Algorithm versus existence:** Solovay–Kitaev supplies an efficient classical compilation algorithm as well as a
  polylog sequence-length bound; state both runtimes if “effective” is the emphasis.
- **Tool naming:** `gridsynth` is implementation-specific and may date; cite a maintained tool or describe exact
  cyclotomic synthesis generally.
- **Temporal precision:** “Actually matters in practice” is unnecessarily exclusive; multi-qubit and other gate-set
  synthesis also matter.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The polylogarithmic surprise is clear, though the exponent history distracts.

#### §8.11 Ross–Selinger specialization and practical conclusion

- **Likely coefficient qualification:** The `$3\log_2(1/\epsilon)$` $T$-count is an expected/typical near-optimal
  ancilla-free bound under number-theoretic conditions/algorithmic randomness; worst-case guarantees may use a
  different leading constant. The manuscript must quote the exact theorem rather than state it universally.
- **Metric:** Specify operator norm/global-phase-insensitive distance and whether approximation is deterministic.
- **Gate sequence wording:** Clifford+$T$ synthesis may use the whole single-qubit Clifford group; saying only “a
  discrete sequence of $H$ and $T$” is algebraically possible because $S=T^2$, but obscures Clifford simplification
  and cost accounting.
- **Asymptotic defect:** Precision compilation **can** inflate algorithmic asymptotic gate complexity when the required
  per-gate error shrinks with input size or circuit length. It often adds logarithmic/polylogarithmic overhead, which
  is still asymptotic overhead even if it preserves broad complexity-class membership.
- **Error budgeting:** Replacing each of $G$ rotations commonly requires local error around
  `$O(\epsilon_{\rm total}/G)$`, leading to a `$G\log(G/\epsilon_{\rm total})$`-type synthesis count. This is the
  practical conclusion readers need.
- **Citations:** Full references are good; align year in prose (“2016”) with preprint/publication distinction.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Contrasting generic and number-theoretic synthesis is highly informative; the final
  no-asymptotic-inflation claim should not undercut it.

#### §8.12 moving-target warning and native-set definition

- **Currency discipline:** The explicit May 2026 snapshot warning is excellent. Because the current review date is July
  2026 and vendor claims are highly unstable, each bullet still needs official-documentation citations and a scheduled
  revalidation mechanism.
- **Physical wording:** Hardware control implements noisy channels generated by driven open-system dynamics, calibrated
  to approximate ideal unitaries. “Whatever unitary the pulses generate” suppresses leakage, decoherence, calibration
  error, and measurement-based primitives.
- **Compiler contract:** A backend target commonly includes parameter ranges, directionality, durations, errors,
  connectivity, calibrations, and conditional operations—not merely a small alphabet. The “native set” definition is a
  useful first approximation.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The candid warning builds trust and frames volatile detail responsibly.

#### §8.12 platform snapshots

- **Superconducting precision:** Virtual `$R_Z$` is typically a frame update with negligible duration/error, not
  literally “free” under every control stack. IBM/Google native entanglers and named processor generations must be
  checked against official 2026 target/basis-gate documentation; vendor-wide “style” masks generation-specific APIs.
- **Fractional-gate claim:** IBM Heron fractional gates, their availability, and their purpose specifically “for
  variational algorithms” are current product claims needing a source; fractional gates may serve broader depth/error
  reduction.
- **Ion overclaim:** High/all-to-all logical connectivity reduces SWAP routing, but “no routing needed” ignores ion
  shuttling, zone capacity, mode crowding, crosstalk, and architecture-specific scheduling.
- **Neutral-atom variability:** Global/local addressability and native blockade operations vary substantially; the
  bullet is appropriately structural but should not imply every QuEra/Pasqal system exposes CZ or generic multiqubit
  gates through a gate-model interface.
- **Photonics category mismatch:** Measurement-based/fusion platforms do not fit a unitary “native gate set” cleanly;
  preparations, fusion measurements, feed-forward, loss handling, and resource-state generation form a different
  compilation model. Calling this out explicitly would improve the chapter’s conceptual breadth.
- **Brand formatting:** “Google/IBM-style,” “IonQ-, Quantinuum-style,” and “QuEra-, Pasqal-style” are awkward and may
  imply endorsement or equivalence. A platform-first table with “examples, not exhaustive” would age better.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The cross-platform comparison is valuable, but its density and volatility call for a table
  with citations/date fields.

#### §8.12 lowering conclusion

- **Correctness:** Connectivity-, fidelity-, and timing-aware lowering is central, and tuning transfers poorly across
  differing targets.
- **Missing constraints:** Directionality, simultaneous-gate conflicts, crosstalk, leakage, measurement/reset latency,
  classical-control latency, and dynamic calibration data are equally material.
- **Interchangeability wording:** A logically equivalent circuit can run on both after recompilation; it is the
  **physical implementation/performance**, not the abstract circuit, that is noninterchangeable.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Sensible practical summary.

#### §8.13 parameterized gates and variational gradients

- **Spelling consistency:** The project alternates “parameterized” and British “parameterised/optimiser.” Choose one
  house style.
- **Parameter-shift conditions:** The displayed rule applies when the differentiated parameter enters once through
  `$e^{-i\theta P/2}$` with two eigenvalues ±1 and other circuit structure is parameter-independent for that
  derivative. Shared/reused parameters require summing occurrence contributions.
- **“Two additional” defect:** The rule needs two shifted circuit expectation evaluations; they are not necessarily
  “additional” to a required unshifted evaluation for the gradient itself. In an optimization loop the baseline cost
  may already be evaluated, but say “two shifted evaluations.”
- **Exactness caveat:** The expectation identity is analytically exact, while finite-shot estimates of each expectation
  are noisy. “Exactly computable” from hardware runs is misleading without distinguishing estimator variance.
- **Observable/cost dependence:** If $O$ or state preparation depends explicitly on $\theta$, extra derivative terms
  appear.
- **Chapter drift:** Earlier Chapter 14 was called amplitude estimation and QFT; here VQE/QAOA are Chapter 15. Verify
  the entire algorithm map.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The gradient formula is an immediate practical payoff.

#### §8.13 Trotterized simulation

- **Major missing-power defect:** A first-order approximation to total evolution is
  `$\left(\prod_k e^{-iH_k t/N}\right)^N$` (with order convention), not just the single-step product displayed. The
  prose calls it a step, but the opening sentence says it approximates `$e^{-iHt}$`; the equation must include or
  explicitly separate the $N$ repetitions.
- **Approximation conditions:** Error depends on commutators, ordering, norms, time, and formula order; the product is
  exact only for commuting terms.
- **Naming:** `$\mathrm{Trotter}_{\Delta t}$` as a made-up operator label is fine if defined, but `$S_1(\Delta t)$` or
  “one product-formula step” is more standard.
- **Parameter role:** Each exponential may still require decomposition into native Pauli rotations; it is not
  automatically a hardware gate.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Good second use case, with a consequential missing repetition.

#### §8.14 single-qubit synthesis

- **Group mismatch:** U(2) requires a global phase in addition to exact Euler SU(2) synthesis; earlier sections handle
  it, but this summary should remain consistent.
- **Algorithm currency:** Modern Clifford+$T$ number-theoretic synthesis is normally preferred over generic
  Solovay–Kitaev for supported targets; ordering them as equivalent alternatives obscures performance.
- **Entertainment: 3/5.** Accurate recap, little new insight.

#### §8.14 two-qubit KAK synthesis

- **Correctness:** Three CNOTs suffice for an arbitrary two-qubit unitary up to local gates, and the Cartan/KAK
  decomposition classifies the nonlocal content.
- **Convention sensitivity:** Canonical-coordinate factors differ by factors of 1/2, Pauli normalization, signs, and
  Weyl chamber choice. The manuscript’s no-half exponent and iSWAP coordinate must be checked together and the chamber
  upper bounds stated.
- **Cost classification:** The `$c_z=0$` face/two-CNOT criterion and one-CNOT local-equivalence class should be cited;
  boundaries have subtleties and native-direction constraints can add gates.
- **“Entangling power” terminology:** KAK coordinates determine local-equivalence class/nonlocal content, while
  “entangling power” is also a specific scalar average measure and does not alone determine CNOT count.
- **Native-cost caveat:** Counting iSWAP/fSim uses, arbitrary single-qubit availability, echo structure, and allowed
  continuous parameters requires a different synthesis theorem—not merely substituting the name in a cost counter.
- **Render risk:** Doubled thin spaces recur.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The Weyl-chamber picture is sophisticated and valuable, though it deserves a diagram.

#### §8.14 multiqubit synthesis and compiler examples

- **Complexity:** Generic exact synthesis has `$\Theta(4^n)$` scale by parameter counting; “at most `$O(4^n)$`” is
  correct but should be paired with a lower bound so readers understand inevitability.
- **Optimization hardness:** “Finding optimal ... is the central problem” is broad; mapping, scheduling, pulse
  synthesis, error mitigation, and verification are also central compilation problems.
- **Duplicate product defect:** `t|ket⟩` and “Quantinuum’s TKET” refer to the same compiler/tool lineage, so the list
  counts it twice. Use one current official name.
- **Tool taxonomy:** Qiskit Transpiler, TKET, and Cirq are not directly parallel labels—frameworks contain multiple
  passes/compilers. Version/date and citations are needed.
- **Objective omissions:** Add estimated infidelity, duration, crosstalk, measurement/reset cost, ancilla count, and
  spacetime volume; raw CNOT count is CNOT-centric for non-CNOT hardware.
- **Temporal stability:** Vendor/tool specifics should inherit the May 2026 snapshot warning.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The jump from Weyl geometry to real compilers is effective despite the duplicate name.

#### §8.14 pipeline takeaway

- **Cross-reference risk:** Verify Chapters 19 and 23.
- **Pulse endpoint overgeneralization:** Photonic/measurement-based platforms named in §8.12 do not end in “a stream of
  pulses sent to physical qubits” in the same sense. Outputs may be optical settings, measurements, schedules, or
  control waveforms.
- **Target-unitary limitation:** Compilers also target channels, measurements, resets, classical control, and pulse-
  level experiments; a unitary is not always one pipeline endpoint.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The long-pipeline image is strong if broadened beyond pulse-controlled qubits.

#### §8.14 sanity checks and footer

- **Checks 1–4:** Algebraically sound and well chosen; check 3’s explicit warning that basis-state global phases become
  relative on superpositions is particularly excellent.
- **Check 4 result:** Both sides create
  `$(|00\rangle+|01\rangle+|10\rangle-|11\rangle)/2$`; including the target answer would aid self-checking.
- **Check 5 impossible as written:** No Toffoli matrix is displayed anywhere in this chapter, yet the reader is asked
  to “derive the ... truth table from its matrix.” Either include the `$8\times8$` matrix, ask for derivation from the
  verbal action, or point to Appendix B.
- **AND caveat:** With target initialized to 0, Toffoli writes `$a\land b$` into the target while preserving controls;
  explicitly require computational-basis inputs.
- **Coverage gaps:** No check exercises controlled global phase, universality approximation, parameter shift, Trotter
  repetition, or KAK coordinates.
- **Navigation:** Footer links are syntactically consistent.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The third check is unusually thoughtful and catches a real phase misconception.

#### Chapter 8 overall verdict

- **Overall:** A broad, energetic gate-and-synthesis chapter that connects matrices to fault tolerance, native
  hardware, and compiler objectives unusually early. The conjugation/measurement distinction, multi-control ancilla
  tradeoff, explicit CHSH-era resource links, and sanity-check phase trap are strong.
- **Strong-defect shortlist:** The Trotter approximation omits the $N$th power; the Bell/QFT chapter cross-reference
  appears inconsistent; Toffoli truth table is requested “from its matrix” although no matrix is supplied; t|ket⟩ and
  TKET are duplicated as separate compilers; all Clifford gates are generalized as transversal/minimal-overhead;
  Hermitian logarithms are identified with hardware-engineered Hamiltonians; Solovay–Kitaev synthesis is said not to
  inflate asymptotic complexity; and gate definitions alternate inconsistently between one/two-qubit primitives and
  arbitrary $n$-qubit unitaries.
- **Recurring slight concerns:** Approximate universality versus exact synthesis and global phase need tighter
  separation; current hardware/vendor claims require official May/July 2026 verification; cost models mix Toffolis,
  elementary gates, CNOTs, and $T$s; “free” Clifford/virtual gates are architecture-dependent; and doubled LaTeX
  escapes remain widespread.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Dense but rarely dry; the chapter succeeds best when a small identity immediately connects
  to an operational or compilation consequence.
- **Second-pass status:** Complete for this manuscript file; the opening, all fourteen numbered sections, every
  theorem/citation/platform bullet/decomposition/sanity check, and footer were read and reviewed separately. Four
  referenced SVGs remain queued for their own one-file asset passes.

### `book/part-04-gates-and-circuits/09-quantum-circuits.md` — second pass

#### Chapter 9 title, status, navigation, opening contract, and reading guide

- **Metaphor:** “Gates are the alphabet; circuits are the sentences” is concise and effective.
- **SDK overclaim:** Not every quantum SDK exposes only or primarily the circuit abstraction; annealing, pulse,
  photonic, analog, and measurement-based platforms expose different intermediate representations.
- **Runability metrics:** Depth, width, and connectivity are central but not sufficient; native gate support, fidelity,
  duration, crosstalk, calibration, measurement/reset, classical latency, and job limits also govern execution.
- **Part-reference risk:** Verify Parts 8 and 9 against the current map.
- **Navigation:** Relative links are syntactically consistent.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The opening promises both visual literacy and practical constraints without delay.

#### §9.1 diagram graph model and glyphs

- **DAG qualification:** A static unitary circuit has an acyclic dependency graph, but diagrams with classical loops,
  repeat-until-success constructs, or dynamic control may represent cyclic programs even if a single unrolled run is
  acyclic. This chapter later introduces dynamic circuits.
- **Box-spanning claim:** A multiqubit gate may be drawn as connected symbols or a box spanning wire positions, but a
  spanning rectangle can visually cover intermediate wires it does not act on. Diagram dialects vary.
- **Classical-wire convention:** Double classical lines are common in older/textbook notation but not near-universal
  in modern SDK renderers; many use single wires with labels/arrows. The paragraph itself should be more cautious.
- **Measurement icon:** A meter/gauge symbol is common; describing it as “D-shaped” may not match the referenced SVG
  or vendor glyphs. Visual verification is needed.
- **Box width:** Often cosmetic in abstract diagrams, but scheduled/pulse-aware diagrams may use width to encode
  duration. Qualify by diagram type.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Glyph decoding is practical and well scoped.

#### §9.1 ordering, time, and endianness conventions

- **Qiskit explanation defect:** Qiskit commonly draws qubit 0 at the top while treating it as the least-significant
  statevector bit; displayed count strings conventionally place the highest classical bit on the left. Saying the top
  LSB is chosen “so it lines up with classical bit-string order” appears backwards or at least deeply ambiguous.
- **Two-versus-three choices:** Wire order, tensor-factor/statevector index significance, classical-register mapping,
  and rendered count-string order are separable conventions. The prose calls them “two independent choices” after
  discussing three; this simplification is the source of many Qiskit errors.
- **Qiskit universalization:** Drawers can reverse bits, registers can map nontrivially, and measurement mapping can
  permute qubits to classical bits. “Qiskit deliberately reverses on both” is not stable across options.
- **Cross-reference inconsistency:** The paragraph points endianness to §4.8, while earlier chapters primarily cited
  §4.2 and Appendix A; verify exact contents.
- **Asset review limitation:** `figures/bell-state.svg` has unusually descriptive alt text but awaits its own one-file
  review.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Endianness warnings are high-value; precision must match their importance.

#### §9.2 layered circuit definition and sampling

- **Definition:** Treating each `$U_\ell$` as a layer/tensor product of disjoint local gates is coherent, but then $L$
  is depth, not general sequential gate count. Say so explicitly.
- **Commutation:** Operators with disjoint support commute, but hardware scheduling may still forbid simultaneous
  execution due to crosstalk/shared controls. Algebraic layerability and physical simultaneity differ.
- **Measurement formula defect:** The text says “a subset” is measured, then gives
  `$P(x)=|\langle x|C|0^n\rangle|^2$`, which is the full-register computational-basis probability. For subset $S$,
  probabilities require summing over unmeasured strings or using projectors/reduced states.
- **Mid-circuit scope:** The definition only covers final measurement and a unitary $C$, while this same chapter
  promises mid-circuit measurement/feed-forward. Present it as the basic static circuit, then generalize to dynamic
  circuits.
- **Input scope:** Algorithms accept arbitrary/classical/quantum inputs, not only initialized zero; “usually” is
  acceptable for algorithm descriptions but should not become the model definition.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Clean mathematical formalization, with the subset/full-measurement inconsistency significant.

#### §9.2 model equivalence claims

- **Adiabatic condition defect:** Polynomial equivalence is not guaranteed merely by a “sufficiently smooth”
  Hamiltonian. Locality, polynomially bounded norms/resources, spectral-gap/runtime conditions, initialization, and
  approximation accuracy matter.
- **Continuous-variable overreach:** “Various continuous-variable schemes” require encoding, squeezing/energy,
  precision, and gate-set assumptions; CV models are not blanket polynomially equivalent under every physical resource
  accounting.
- **MBQC condition:** A suitable universal cluster resource, adaptive measurements, and classical feed-forward give
  equivalence; naming a 2D cluster state is directionally correct.
- **Category error:** Qiskit/Cirq are SDKs/frameworks; OpenQASM and Quil are languages/IRs; TKET is a compiler/toolkit.
  Listing all as “major SDKs” is taxonomically wrong and repeats Chapter 8’s tool confusion.
- **Universality citation:** Each equivalence is a theorem-level claim and needs citations.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The model landscape is useful but far too compressed for the strength of “polynomially
  equivalent.”

#### §9.2 what the circuit model is not

- **Analog boundary:** Gates may represent discretized analog evolution, and pulse-level circuit schedules can expose
  Hamiltonian control. The basic circuit abstraction hides it; saying it “is not a description” is fair only at that
  layer.
- **Interference wording:** Amplitudes propagate linearly and interference appears when paths/components recombine;
  “between gates” is vague but the probability/amplitude distinction is correct.
- **Decomposition count:** With continuous parameters and insertion of identities, a unitary has infinitely many
  circuit descriptions, not merely exponentially many. Under bounded discrete libraries/length, counts can be
  exponential. State the constraints.
- **Optimization cause:** Compilation is hard not just because many decompositions exist, but because optimal synthesis
  and constrained mapping are combinatorial/continuous optimization problems.
- **Cross-reference risk:** Verify Chapter 23 and §9.13.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The three negations effectively prevent common category mistakes.

#### §9.3 ancilla definition and three idioms

- **Definition nuance:** Ancillas are auxiliary subsystems with specified initialization/return conditions; not all are
  scratch space, and discarding/resetting is safe only when the algorithm’s channel semantics permit it.
- **Oracle resource defect:** An arbitrary classical circuit of size $T$ can be reversibly embedded with various
  time–space tradeoffs; `$O(T)$` workspace is a straightforward history construction, not an unavoidable “cost.”
  Efficient reversible compilation may use much fewer ancillas at greater recomputation cost.
- **Function-register width:** `$y\oplus f(x)$` requires $y$ to match the output width and XOR to be defined bitwise;
  state this for non-Boolean functions.
- **Phase-kickback description:** Correct central idea, but the control receives an eigenphase only when the target is a
  known eigenstate and controlled-$U$ is applied. A one-line equation would prevent slogan-only understanding.
- **Chapter drift:** QFT and phase estimation are both sent to Chapter 14, which earlier served amplitude estimation and
  conflicts with Chapter 8’s QFT reference. This is likely systematic numbering drift.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The three idioms make ancillas feel functional rather than merely extra wires.

#### §9.3 physical cost paragraph

- **Temporal/hardware claim:** “Each logical ancilla ... hundreds of physical qubits” varies by code distance,
  architecture, target error, lattice-surgery layout, and whether it is a patch, factory workspace, or temporary
  logical degree. It may be far more or less; cite a resource model.
- **NISQ terminology:** “Current NISQ hardware” is a moving and contested label. Ancillas also increase depth, reset,
  readout, routing, and error exposure, not only capacity/crosstalk.
- **Metaphor:** “Paying rent” is memorable and accurate as resource-accounting intuition.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** Excellent closing phrase.

#### §9.4 uncomputation pattern

- **Overclaim:** Discarding a correlated ancilla makes the retained state mixed only when quantum/classical
  correlations are present in the relevant superposition; measuring it can condition rather than simply “collapse or
  decohere” the computation if the result is retained and corrected. The danger is loss of coherence between branches
  distinguished by garbage.
- **Uncompute precondition:** Applying the inverse after copying works only if the intervening operation preserves the
  data needed by `$U_{\rm compute}^\dagger$`; copying an unknown quantum state is forbidden, while copying a
  computational-basis function value via XOR is allowed.
- **Diagram order:** Function-composition right-to-left is mathematically standard, but a circuit chapter should also
  show left-to-right wire order to avoid reversing compute/copy/uncompute.
- **Reset alternative:** Returning to $|0\rangle$ unitarily is one fix; measurement-based uncomputation and reset with
  feed-forward can reduce cost in suitable cases, as §9.5 hints.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** The compute–copy–uncompute pattern is one of the chapter’s most important reusable ideas.

#### §9.4 Bennett construction and interference

- **Complexity precision:** Reversible simulation bounds depend on the original gate model, fanout, retained inputs,
  depth, and pebbling strategy. Saying a size-$T$, depth-$d$ circuit becomes size `$O(T)$` while “at the cost of extra
  time or extra ancillae” is internally vague: the simple history construction already specifies both. Give one
  explicit bound then mention tradeoffs.
- **Output condition:** The clean result is generally `$|x\rangle|f(x)\rangle|0\cdots0\rangle$`, with input retained
  because reversibility forbids erasing it.
- **No-garbage overstatement:** Interference only requires garbage states associated with branches intended to
  interfere to have sufficient overlap/indistinguishability. Not every algorithm requires a global invariant that all
  ancillas always be zero.
- **Discarding nuance:** Tracing entangled garbage yields mixed reduced state, but intended output can itself be mixed
  or can tolerate garbage orthogonal to measured structure.
- **False universal:** Not “every” phase-kickback-style algorithm relies on clean uncomputation; phase estimation often
  measures/discards registers in designed ways. Identify specific oracle constructions instead.
- **Bennett citation:** Historical attribution needs a formal reference.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Interference gives uncomputation a genuine reason, though the invariant is too absolute.

#### §9.5 compute–copy–uncompute garbage idiom

- **Major tensor-factor defect:** After reversible evaluation on a superposition, the clean state is generally
  `$\sum_x\alpha_x|x\rangle|f(x)\rangle|0\rangle_{\rm work}$`; the **workspace** is a clean tensor factor, but the
  output `$f(x)$` is usually entangled/correlated with the input and is not itself a clean independent tensor factor.
- **Copy terminology:** XORing a classical function value encoded in the computational basis is reversible fanout, not
  cloning an arbitrary quantum state. State this explicitly.
- **Garbage definition:** Garbage is unwanted output correlated with live data; entanglement is neither necessary
  (classical correlation can leak which-path information) nor by itself “pollution.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Highly reusable pattern, with one technically important phrase reversed.

#### §9.5 catalytic and measurement-based release

- **Catalyst guarantee:** Declaring an ancilla “must return” does not causally prevent traces; correct catalytic
  constructions are designed/proved to restore it. Approximate catalysts and correlated catalysis add further nuance.
- **Terminology/citation:** Catalytic quantum computation is advanced and ambiguous here. Give a concrete gate-
  synthesis example or defer it rather than leave a one-sentence teaser.
- **Measurement release:** Correct in teleportation/measurement-based uncomputation, provided outcomes are retained and
  conditional corrections/adaptations remove their effect on surviving data.
- **Feed-forward condition:** Pauli corrections can often be tracked in a frame rather than applied with immediate
  physical low-latency feed-forward.
- **Unsupported superlative:** “Most quantum-algorithm bugs” reducing to two ancilla mistakes is unmeasured and
  implausibly broad. Recast as “two common textbook derivation bugs.”
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Garbage collection is an excellent software analogy; catalytic computation needs more payoff.

#### §9.6 measurement primitive and basis changes

- **Destructive contradiction:** The paragraph calls measurement “destructive” and then says the qubit remains in the
  outcome eigenstate, which describes ideal projective/QND state update, not physical destruction. Separate
  destructive detector readout from nonunitary collapse.
- **Register output:** Measuring an $m$-qubit register produces an $m$-bit outcome in the simple computational PVM, not
  generically a bit string for every measurement primitive.
- **Arbitrary-axis rotation:** The needed unitary maps the measurement eigenbasis/axis to $Z$; sign and adjoint
  direction must be specified.
- **Pauli-string readout:** Local basis rotations plus individual $Z$ measurements produce a parity/product eigenvalue,
  not XOR “modulo signs” without mapping bits `$b_i$` to eigenvalues `$(-1)^{b_i}$`. It also reveals more information
  than a nondemolition collective Pauli measurement.
- **Identity factors:** Qubits where `$P_i=I$` need not be measured.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Useful measurement cookbook, with destructive terminology likely to confuse the next section.

#### §9.6 shot noise and expectation estimation

- **Variance formula:** For i.i.d. direct measurements of observable $A$, the sample-mean variance is
  `$\operatorname{Var}_\psi(A)/N$`. A generic noncommuting observable often must be decomposed into measurable terms,
  adding grouping/allocation/covariance overhead.
- **One-bit qualification:** A binary single-qubit PVM yields one bit; generalized measurements can have more outcomes
  and hardware produces analog readout before discrimination.
- **State notation:** Mixed/noisy experiments require `$\operatorname{tr}(\rho A)$`, not only
  `$\langle\psi|A|\psi\rangle$`.
- **Shot-noise “floor”:** Increasing shots lowers sampling variance; hardware drift and noise can impose other floors.
  It is a cost/scaling, not a nonzero irreducible floor.
- **Part-reference risk:** Verify Part 6.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** Good practical consequence.

#### §9.7 deferred-measurement statement

- **Scope overclaim:** Standard deferred measurement applies when measurement outcomes only classically control later
  quantum operations and can be coherently stored. General adaptive measurements, resets/reuse, postselection,
  variable-length loops, and externally emitted outcomes may require ancillas/reversible classical logic or cannot be
  replaced by the simple same-wire control described.
- **Basis:** A noncomputational/general measurement must first be coherently dilated or basis-rotated; “any
  measurement” is too broad.
- **Resource cost:** Deferral may require extra qubits to retain each outcome, not only keeping the measured qubit live.
- **Channel-order confusion:** Under standard right-to-left composition, the two channel expressions do not align
  clearly with the prose’s “first case/second case,” and `$\mathrm{Meas}_{\rm ctrl}$` is undefined. Draw a two-wire
  equality instead.
- **Equivalence target:** The circuits match final classical output statistics (and selected unmeasured outputs under
  appropriate dephasing), not necessarily every joint quantum state.
- **Entertainment: 4/5.** The principle is important; formalism currently makes it harder rather than clearer.

#### §9.7 theory-versus-engineering tradeoff

- **“Typically” overclaim:** Early measurement can free/reuse qubits but adds readout/reset latency and error, may stall
  coherent qubits, and may be slower than coherent control. Hardware-dependent compilation chooses, not typically one
  direction.
- **Decoherence tradeoff:** Deferral increases live time/width; early measurement introduces classical latency. This is
  an excellent tradeoff if stated symmetrically.
- **Equivalence wording:** Resource-unconstrained ideal equivalence does not imply equal noise, latency, fault-
  tolerance, or hardware behavior.
- **Entertainment: 5/5.** “Theory says equivalent; engineering chooses” is a strong framing.

#### §9.8 moving-target warning and physical readout

- **Currency:** May 2026 platform support claims require official per-device citations and rechecking on the July 2026
  review date.
- **QND terminology:** Nondemolition means repeated ideal measurement of the observable preserves its value, not merely
  that a physical carrier “survives.” Real readout can cause leakage/state transitions while remaining reusable after
  reset.
